- Current API price ladder (output $/M tokens, log scale): GPT-5.5 Pro $180, Fable 5.1 / GPT-6 Astra $50, Opus 5.5 $20, Gemini 4 Argon $10→$20, GPT-6 Luna / Haiku 5.5 $0.50, gpt-5-nano / Gemini 2.5 Flash-Lite $0.40. Data: official pricing pages accessed 2026-11-07
- Epoch decline rate by 'age' of capability: 66%/q at SOTA → 32%/q after 2 years. Data: Epoch plunging price of thought
- Big-4 hyperscaler capex 2025 (~$410B) vs 2026 (~$720-750B) by company, with Alphabet's 2027 >$300B signal. Data: company Q3 2026 8-Ks / CNBC
- Data-centre electricity 2025 485 TWh → 2030 ≈950 TWh, with the AI-focused share tripling. Data: IEA Key Questions on Energy and AI
- Output tokens per answer over time, reasoning vs non-reasoning (5x/yr vs 2.2x/yr). Data: epoch.ai/data-insights/output-length

### open_questions

- Search budget exhausted mid-run (200-search limit): the following could not be checked against primary pages and need a follow-up pass.
- Does the Stanford AI Index 2026 (released ~13 Apr 2026) contain an updated inference-cost-at-fixed-capability series? I could not access the 2026 PDF (a guessed URL returned 404). Secondary sites mostly repeat the 2025 figure.
- ARC Prize 2026 cost-per-task figures (DeepSeek V4 Flash $0.01-0.02, Grok 4.6 $0.30, Gemini 3.7 Flash $0.25, Fable 5.1 $3.12 vs $4.49) come from secondary blogs or X snippets. They need confirming on arcprize.org model pages. X returned HTTP 402.
- What does the 'Cost' column on arcprize.org/leaderboard represent ($26.1K etc.)? Most likely ARC-AGI-3 total run cost, not per-task. This needs confirming before any use.
- Microsoft's capex guidance basis: CNBC says '$170 billion for all of calendar 2027', which may be a misstatement (calendar 2026?). Verify against the FQ1 FY27 transcript.
- Epoch 2025 blog's reported median (≈50x/yr, rising to 200x/yr after Jan 2024) appears in secondary summaries but was not visible in the page excerpt I read.
- There is no 2026 primary update of Epoch's output-length (tokens-per-answer) growth, so whether the 5x/yr trend continued is unknown.
- There is no direct measure of total enterprise or consumer spend on inference in 2026 (e.g. OpenRouter token volumes, lab revenues), which would show whether falling unit prices are outweighed by volume (Jevons effect). Only Google's ~32B tokens/min (>4x y/y) was found.
- IEA 2025 AI-focused vs non-AI split (155/330 TWh) appears only in a secondary snippet.
- TrendForce DRAM figures and GPU rental indexes came via secondary aggregators. Primary TrendForce / SemiAnalysis data was not accessed.

## superhuman

The evidence supports a narrower version of belief (2): AI clearly beats top humans or typical experts on a set of narrow, well-scored tasks, and the set grew a lot from 2025 to 2026. Math: AI went from gold-level 35/42 at IMO 2025 (Google's result was officially certified, OpenAI's was self-graded) to two officially graded perfect 42/42 scores at IMO 2026, from Huawei Celia and Xiaohongshu dots-note-3.0. Only 7 of 666 human contestants scored 42/42. Competitive programming: OpenAI solved 12/12 at the ICPC 2025 World Finals, while the best human team solved 11. OpenAI placed 6th of 330 humans at IOI 2025. The strongest evidence is that AI is now solving open problems in research math, not just contest problems. In May 2026 an OpenAI model disproved Erdős's 1946 unit-distance conjecture; Noga Alon, Tim Gowers and Thomas Bloom backed the result and it was published as arXiv:2605.20695. There are several other verified Erdős-problem results. On structured multiple-choice expert benchmarks, AI is past the human-expert baseline: GPQA Diamond scores of about 90-96% vs 65-81% for PhD-level validators. Applied areas also show superhuman or better-than-human results: AI weather models (GenCast beat the ECMWF ensemble, ENS, on 97.2% of 1,320 targets; Google DeepMind's hurricane-track model beat NHC's official forecasts in 2025), Waymo's crash rates (95% fewer serious-injury crashes than a human benchmark, Waymo-reported), complex diagnostic vignettes (MAI-DxO 85.5% vs 20% for physicians, under restrictive conditions), and classic games. Forecasting is at parity, not superhuman: FRI says the best AI is statistically indistinguishable from superforecasters (as of July 2026). There is real counter-evidence for the author's framing. Real-world end-to-end work is still mostly sub-human: the best AI completes only about 21% of real freelance projects on Scale's Remote Labor Index. Robots are 2-10x slower than humans and fail at multi-step household tasks. ARC-AGI-3 launched in March 2026 with frontier models under 1% vs humans at 100%. It then jumped to 30-63% (and up to about 97-99% in other configurations) by Sept-Oct 2026, but the official pages conflict on which number is the headline. Many 2026 leaderboard numbers come from aggregators or vendors, so they need checking against the source. For belief (3): the jaggedness itself (perfect IMO scores while still failing many real jobs) is the strongest argument that a single 'human-level' line is not a useful yardstick.

### Fact-check verdict

Mostly sound and well caveated, but several claims are overstated or have the wrong source. Confirmed: IMO 2025, the ICPC/DeepMind figures, IOI 2025 (vendor-reported), the unit-distance disproof (arXiv:2605.20695 with the nine named authors), the Quanta Erdős figures, the ForecastBench p-values and date, the MASAI rates, the MAI-DxO accuracies, and Waymo's September 2026 figures (vendor). Corrections: (1) IMO 2026 'officially graded' is company-reported only, with no IMO statement; only Xiaohongshu says it submitted to organisers; at least four other labs' models claimed 42/42 under unofficial grading; and the time-window, no-intervention and 29-point cutoff details are unsupported. (2) GenCast's 97.2% and 99.8% are Nature figures, but the cited arXiv says 97.4%. (3) GPQA's 65%/34% are on the 448-question main set, not 'Extended'. Diamond and AI scores need primary citations, and the 96% figures are aggregator numbers. (4) ForecastBench's 'Jan 2026 0.017 Brier' and the Gowers quote are not in the cited sources. Not verified: the Pakistan RCT effect sizes (paywalled; preprint and published numbers mixed) and the hurricane nmi and km figures (secondary source, Franklin's unofficial analysis). The search budget ran out partway, so the verification used direct fetches of the cited URLs only.

- **confirmed**: IMO 2025: Gemini Deep Think and an OpenAI experimental model both scored 35/42 (P1-P5 solved, P6 zero). Only Google's result was officially certified; OpenAI's was graded by three former IMO medalists it hired. 5 humans scored 42/42. → 35/42 for both. Gemini was graded and certified by IMO coordinators. OpenAI was not an official participant. The count of 5 human perfect scores was not re-verified this session. (https://simonwillison.net/2025/Jul/21/gemini-imo/). Willison confirms 35/42 for both, P6 failed, and Gemini's official certification (Hassabis called it 'the first official gold-level performance grading'). The page does not name OpenAI's graders. 'Three former IMO medalists' matches OpenAI researchers' own posts, which I did not re-fetch. Willison's only human figure is that 6 of 630 contestants got full marks on P6, so the '5 perfect 42/42 scores' figure needs the imo-official.org 2025 results page. Cite the primary DeepMind post, not a blog.
- **corrected**: IMO 2026: Huawei Celia and Xiaohongshu dots-note-3.0 each received officially graded 42/42. Problems were given after the human exam, with a fixed time window and no human intervention. 7 of 666 humans scored 42/42. Gold cutoff 29. → Both reportedly scored 42/42. Only Xiaohongshu says it submitted to IMO organisers for grading; AFP does not say who graded Huawei's model. 7 of 666 humans scored perfect (per the official scoreboard, as cited by AFP). AFP also reports that Deedy Das said OpenAI, Anthropic, Axiom Math and Moonshot Kimi K3 models scored 42/42 in his own unofficial grading. The 'fixed time window / no human intervention' conditions and the 29-point gold cutoff are not supported by AFP or the dots page. (https://www.france24.com/en/live-news/20260723-ai-catches-up-with-humans-to-score-100-at-top-maths-contest). 'Officially graded' is a company claim: there is no IMO official quote or imo-official.org statement. The dots page says it took part at the IMO Organizing Committee's invitation and received 'official marking organized by the committee'. It says no human translated or formalized the problems, but it gives no time limit. The briefing must not say only two AIs reached 42/42: at least four more claimed it under self-grading. Say 'company-reported official grading'.
- **confirmed**: ICPC 2025 World Finals: OpenAI 12/12 and the best human team 11. Gemini 2.5 Deep Think 10/12 in 677 minutes, which would place 2nd of 139, and it solved Problem C, which no university team solved. → Gemini 10/12, 677 combined minutes, would rank 2nd vs 139 university teams, and solved Problem C within 30 minutes (no human team solved it). DeepMind post dated 2025-09-17. OpenAI's 12/12 is OpenAI-reported. (https://deepmind.google/blog/gemini-achieves-gold-medal-level-at-the-international-collegiate-programming-contest-world-finals/). The DeepMind page does not mention OpenAI or give the top human team's solve count. OpenAI's 12/12 and the human best of 11 come from OpenAI and press, not ICPC. Label them vendor-reported, with ICPC only confirming that the solutions were accepted. I could not verify the claim that the ICPC 2026 World Finals were postponed to April 2027. Drop it or source it.
- **confirmed**: IOI 2025: OpenAI placed 6th of 330 humans under the same 5-hour, 50-submission rules (98th percentile; 49th percentile in 2024). → Ahead of all but 5 of 330 human participants, 98th percentile, 49th percentile in 2024, same 5-hour and 50-submission limits. (https://the-decoder.com/openais-ai-system-wins-a-gold-medal-level-score-at-the-international-olympiad-in-informatics-2025/). Vendor-reported: the source is OpenAI researcher Sheryl Hsu's X post. The 2024 percentile is unsourced in the article. The IOI did not certify the result. The researcher labeled it correctly.
- **confirmed**: An OpenAI general-purpose reasoning model disproved Erdős's 1946 unit-distance conjecture. It was endorsed by Alon, Wood and Bloom, and nine mathematicians including Alon and Gowers published a verified simplified version (arXiv:2605.20695). → arXiv:2605.20695, 'Remarks on the disproof of the unit distance conjecture', submitted 2026-05-20 by Alon, Bloom, Gowers, Litt, Sawin, Shankar, Tsimerman, Wang and Wood. The abstract refers to 'the recent OpenAI-generated counterexample'. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20695). TechCrunch confirms the 1946 conjecture, the general-purpose model, the endorsement by Alon, Wood and Bloom, and the October 2025 Kevin Weil/GPT-5 episode. TechCrunch does not quote Gowers, so the Gowers line 'no previous AI proof had come close' needs its own source. 'First AI disproof of a prominent open conjecture central to a field' is the endorsers' or OpenAI's framing; attribute it. Quanta confirms that humans substantially improved on the result within weeks.
- **confirmed**: Erdős problems in 2026: DeepMind (Jan) screened 700, solved 4 and found forgotten solutions to 9; DeepMind (May) resolved 9 of 353; the database shows 565 solved / 652 open; #728 via GPT-5.2 Pro + Aristotle; #1196 in a Tao co-authored paper; Alon stopped; Astra solved three more. → All the figures match Quanta (2026-08-03). The May DeepMind search covered only problems already written in formal logic. Astra was announced 2026-08-01 with 10 mathematical advances, including three Erdős problems. (https://www.quantamagazine.org/why-the-legendary-erdos-problems-are-falling-to-ai-20260803/). Quanta says Kevin Barreto and Liam Price used GPT-5.2 Pro on #728 and Aristotle checked the proof. 'Essentially autonomously' is a characterization, so attribute it. Astra is vendor-reported and its problem numbers (#146, #180, #183) were not verified. The database counts are a snapshot as of 2026-08-03 and include problems solved without AI.
- **corrected**: GPQA human baselines: experts 65% (74% after discounting clear mistakes), non-experts 34%. On Diamond, experts 81.3% and non-experts 22.1%. OpenAI's PhD experts 69.7%. Top AI on Diamond about 90-96%. → The 65%, 74% and 34% figures (448 questions) are confirmed from the arXiv abstract. The Diamond 81.3% / 22.1% figures and OpenAI's 69.7% were not visible on the abstract page. Top-AI figures of 96% come only from aggregators. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12022). Note that 65% and 34% are on the 448-question main set, not 'Extended' (546), so fix the label. The Diamond human numbers must be cited to the paper's table, and the 69.7% to OpenAI's o1 post (vendor). The 'AI ~90-96%' range mixes Epoch-verified figures (~87-90%) with aggregator and vendor numbers. Use Epoch's own figure as the headline. This is a saturated multiple-choice benchmark, not expert work.
- **confirmed**: MAI-DxO (o3) correctly diagnosed 85.5% of 304 NEJM cases vs 20% for 21 physicians. 85.5% at $7,184; 79.9% at $2,397; physicians at $2,963. → 304 NEJM-CPC cases, 85.5% in the max-accuracy configuration, ~80% while cutting costs 20% vs physicians, physicians averaged 20%. arXiv v1 2025-06-27, v2 2025-07-02. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.22405). The abstract confirms the accuracies but not the dollar figures or the 21-physician / 12-year details. Those are in the paper body and were not re-verified. It is a vendor-authored preprint, physicians were restricted from outside resources, and the cases are hard teaching puzzles. The researcher's caveats are correct.
- **unsupported**: Pakistan RCT (Nature Health): the LLM alone beat LLM-assisted physicians by 11.5 pp (95% CI 5.5-17.5), and LLM-assisted physicians beat conventional-resources physicians by about 34 points. → Not verified. The Nature URL redirects to an auth wall, and the search budget was exhausted. (https://www.nature.com/articles/s44360-025-00007-8). The researcher admits the figures come from search snippets, and the '~34 points' is a preprint figure, so don't mix preprint and published numbers. The n=60, the effect sizes and the 2026-02 date all need checking against the published paper's abstract before publication. Outcome was a reasoning/diagnosis score on vignettes, not real patients.
- **confirmed**: MASAI: interval cancers 1.55 vs 1.76 per 1,000 (ratio 0.88, CI 0.65-1.18), 29% higher detection, 44% workload reduction, N=105,934. → 1.55 (95% CI 1.23-1.92) vs 1.76 (1.42-2.15) per 1,000; ratio 0.88 (0.65-1.18), P=.41. Sensitivity 80.5% vs 73.8% (P=.031), specificity 98.5% in both arms. 44% workload reduction (Lancet Oncology interim), 29% more cancers detected. (https://ascopost.com/news/february-2026/randomized-trial-shows-ai-supported-mammography-improves-sensitivity-and-lowers-interval-cancer-rate/). N=105,934 was not stated in ASCO Post, so verify it in the Lancet paper. ASCO Post is secondary; cite The Lancet directly. The sensitivity gain (80.5% vs 73.8%) was missed and is the significant result. The comparison is AI plus a radiologist vs double reading, not AI alone.
- **confirmed**: ForecastBench (FRI, 2026-07-16): best AI statistically indistinguishable from superforecasters. Cassi p=0.41, xAI 0.15-0.16, GDM 0.14. First AI above the superforecaster median on market questions. Superforecasters led by 0.017 Brier in Jan 2026. → Post dated 2026-07-16. Cassi p=0.41, xAI 0.16/0.15, Google DeepMind 0.14. Cassi ranks above the superforecaster median on market questions, the first model to do so. (https://forecastingresearch.substack.com/p/ai-models-have-likely-reached-parity). The 'January 2026: superforecasters led by 0.017 Brier' figure is not in this post, so source it separately or drop it. The post also says an anonymous ensemble ranks above superforecasters on market questions and that Torchcast leads the dataset-question board. FRI's caveats are confirmed: the 2024 baseline is extrapolated, the CIs overlap, and the benchmark is not saturated. The result is parity, not superhuman.
- **corrected**: GenCast beat ECMWF ENS on 97.2% of 1,320 targets (99.8% at >36h). 15-day ensemble in ~8 minutes on one TPU v5. → The cited arXiv abstract says 97.4% of 1,320 targets, in 8 minutes. 97.2% and 99.8% (>36h) are the Nature 2024 published figures. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.15796). The researcher cites the arXiv URL but quotes the Nature numbers. Cite Nature (Price et al. 2024/2025, doi 10.1038/s41586-024-08252-9) for 97.2%/99.8%. TPU v5 hardware is not in the abstract. The comparison is against a physics ensemble (the older, lower-resolution ENS), not human forecasters.
- **unsupported**: 2025 Atlantic season: GDM cyclone model beat NHC official and all physics models on track (72-h: GDMI 66.5 nmi vs NHC 81.4, ECMWF 102.7, GFS 128.6). Nature paper: 5-day track error 230 km vs 370 km ENS. NHC leaned on it in 2026. → Not verified this session. The Nature paper is behind an auth redirect and the NPR fetch timed out. (https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-026-10953-2). The 72-h numbers come via a secondary source (Horizon Search) citing James Franklin's unofficial verification. That is not NHC's official Tropical Cyclone Report verification, which comes out in spring. 'Beat NHC official' is credible and widely reported, but the specific nmi values need Franklin's primary post or NPR. The 230 km vs 370 km figures and the paper date need the Nature abstract. Keep confidence at medium or lower.
- **confirmed**: Waymo: over 270M rider-only miles through June 2026; 95% fewer serious-injury-or-worse crashes, 82% fewer injury crashes, 93% fewer pedestrian-injury and 86% fewer cyclist-injury crashes. → Page dated 2026-09-24. Over 270M fully autonomous miles through end of June 2026. 95% fewer serious-injury-or-worse crashes (~20x), 82% fewer injury crashes, 93% fewer pedestrian-injury and 86% fewer cyclist-injury crashes. No CIs on the page. (https://waymo.com/blog/shorts/safetydata-september26/). Vendor-reported against Waymo's own constructed human benchmark, so label it that way. I did not re-verify the peer-reviewed 56.7M-mile paper (Traffic Injury Prevention 2025). The 2026 recall/pause should be checked before using this as the headline example of 'superhuman'.

### Missed evidence (from the fact-checker)

- IMO 2026: AFP/France 24 reports that Deedy Das claimed 42/42 for OpenAI, Anthropic, Axiom Math and Moonshot Kimi K3 models under his own unofficial grading. Two officially graded perfect scores is not the whole picture; more frontier labs claim perfect scores. https://www.france24.com/en/live-news/20260723-ai-catches-up-with-humans-to-score-100-at-top-maths-contest
- IMO 2026: no imo-official.org or IMO Board statement endorsing AI grading was found. The 'official marking' claim comes only from company pages (e.g. https://studio-dots-ai.github.io/dots_imo_2026/en.html).
- MASAI: the statistically significant result is higher sensitivity, 80.5% vs 73.8% (P=.031), with equal specificity of 98.5%. The researcher left it out. https://ascopost.com/news/february-2026/randomized-trial-shows-ai-supported-mammography-improves-sensitivity-and-lowers-interval-cancer-rate/
- ForecastBench: an anonymous ensemble and Torchcast submissions also lead parts of the leaderboard, so Cassi is not the only system near or above superforecasters. FRI explicitly says the CIs overlap and the benchmark is not saturated. https://forecastingresearch.substack.com/p/ai-models-have-likely-reached-parity
- Erdős: Quanta notes the May 2026 DeepMind 9-of-353 search was limited to formally stated problems, a selection effect. Astra's 'three more Erdős problems' were part of 10 claimed advances, all vendor-reported. https://www.quantamagazine.org/why-the-legendary-erdos-problems-are-falling-to-ai-20260803/
- Process note: the shared web-search budget was exhausted, so this check used direct fetches of the cited URLs only. The Remote Labor Index (~21%), ARC-AGI-3 figures, robotics claims, and the GPQA Diamond AI scores from the summary were not independently checked and should be verified before publication. The user can send a follow-up to continue searching, or raise CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION.

### Findings

- [high/reputable_press] **At IMO 2025, Google DeepMind's Gemini Deep Think and an OpenAI experimental model both scored gold-level 35/42 (P1-P5 solved, P6 zero). Only Google's result was graded and certified through the official IMO process; OpenAI's was graded by three former IMO medalists it hired.**: 35/42 points (gold cutoff 35). 5 human contestants scored 42/42 in 2025. (2025-07-19 to 2025-07-21). [Google DeepMind blog (Gemini Deep Think IMO gold); press coverage e.g. Simon Willison, Euronews](https://simonwillison.net/2025/Jul/21/gemini-imo/). The primary DeepMind post is at deepmind.google (not fetched here). Do not describe OpenAI's 2025 result as 'officially certified'. In 2025 AI was gold-level but NOT better than the best humans (five humans scored perfect).
- [medium/reputable_press] **At IMO 2026 (Shanghai), Huawei's Celia and Xiaohongshu's dots-note-3.0 each received officially graded perfect scores. Problems were given after the human exam, with a fixed time window and no human intervention.**: 42/42 (100%). Only 7 of 666 human contestants scored 42/42. Gold cutoff reportedly 29/42. (2026-07-15/16 exams; results announced 2026-07-23). [AFP via France 24: 'AI catches up with humans to score 100% at top maths contest'; dots team page](https://www.france24.com/en/live-news/20260723-ai-catches-up-with-humans-to-score-100-at-top-maths-contest). The companies announced the 'official grading' themselves. I found no imo-official.org statement, and AFP quotes no IMO official. The dots page: https://studio-dots-ai.github.io/dots_imo_2026/en.html. The 29-point gold cutoff comes from a secondary blog (digitalapplied.com).
- [low/secondary_aggregator] **At IMO 2026, models from OpenAI, Anthropic, Axiom Math and Moonshot (Kimi K3) also reached 42/42 in an informal test run by VC Deedy Das. These were not official entries, and the graders were Claude-based AI agents. Some of the 42/42 scores came only after 'repair rounds'.**: 42/42 self-administered. The README itself warns 'treat scores as strong but not authoritative'. (2026-07). [AFP/France24; github.com/deedy/imo-2026 (as described by digitalapplied.com)](https://www.digitalapplied.com/blog/imo-2026-perfect-scores-ai-benchmark-saturation). Do not report these as official IMO results. Secondary sources report that NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Ultra scored 30/42 under IMO grading (not verified).
- [high/primary] **At the ICPC 2025 World Finals (Baku), OpenAI's system solved all 12 problems within the 5-hour limit. The best human team solved 11. Gemini 2.5 Deep Think solved 10/12, which would have placed 2nd of 139 teams, and it solved Problem C, which no university team solved.**: OpenAI 12/12 (11 solved on the first submission). Gemini 10/12 in 677 combined minutes. Top human team 11/12. (2025-09-04 (contest); DeepMind post 2025-09-17). [Google DeepMind: Gemini achieves gold-medal level at the ICPC World Finals](https://deepmind.google/blog/gemini-achieves-gold-medal-level-at-the-international-collegiate-programming-contest-world-finals/). ICPC confirmed the solutions were accepted, but its review 'did not validate the system, processes, or underlying model'. The AI ran in a separate experimental track. The OpenAI 12/12 figure is from OpenAI and press (the-decoder.com). The ICPC 2026 World Finals were postponed to April 2027, so no 2026 result exists.
- [medium/vendor_reported] **In the IOI 2025 online AI track, OpenAI's ensemble of general reasoning models scored above all but 5 of 330 human contestants. It was under the same 5-hour, 50-submission rules.**: 6th of 330 humans (gold-medal level; ~98th percentile). The 2024 result was ~49th percentile. (2025-08). [The Decoder (relaying OpenAI researchers' posts)](https://the-decoder.com/openais-ai-system-wins-a-gold-medal-level-score-at-the-international-olympiad-in-informatics-2025/). Not superhuman: 5 humans did better. I found no verified IOI 2026 AI result. A NVIDIA Nemotron 'gold at IOI 2026' claim appeared only in secondary press.
- [high/reputable_press] **An OpenAI general-purpose reasoning model disproved Erdős's 1946 unit-distance conjecture by finding a new family of constructions that beats square-grid configurations. Leading mathematicians backed the result.**: First AI disproof of a prominent open conjecture central to a field. Endorsed by Noga Alon, Melanie Wood and Thomas Bloom. Nine mathematicians including Alon and Gowers published a verified, simplified version (arXiv:2605.20695). (2026-05-20). [TechCrunch: OpenAI claims it solved an 80-year-old math problem — for real this time; Quanta Magazine 2026-08-03](https://techcrunch.com/2026/05/20/openai-claims-it-solved-an-80-year-old-math-problem-for-real-this-time/). Context: in October 2025 OpenAI's Kevin Weil wrongly claimed GPT-5 had solved 10 Erdős problems; it had only found existing literature, and Bloom called it a 'dramatic misrepresentation'. Quanta reports human mathematicians improved on the AI result within weeks. Gowers: no previous AI proof had come close.
- [high/reputable_press] **Many Erdős problems have fallen to AI-assisted work in 2026, but with caveats: some 'solutions' were rediscoveries of old literature, problems vary hugely in difficulty, and many AI proofs go unchecked.**: DeepMind (Jan 2026): screened 700 open conjectures, solved 4 and found forgotten solutions to 9. DeepMind (May 2026): an agent resolved 9 of 353 open problems at a few hundred dollars each. Bloom's database: 565 solved / 652 open (many solved without AI). Problem #728 was solved essentially autonomously (GPT-5.2 Pro + Harmonic's Aristotle, Lean-verified). #1196 was resolved in a paper co-authored by Terence Tao. (2026-01 to 2026-08). [Quanta Magazine: Why the Legendary Erdős Problems Are Falling to AI](https://www.quantamagazine.org/why-the-legendary-erdos-problems-are-falling-to-ai-20260803/). Noga Alon reportedly stopped working on Erdős problems because AI now solves them. On 2026-08-01 OpenAI reported its unreleased 'Astra' model solved three more (e.g. #146, #180, #183); I did not verify these independently. arXiv writeup of #728: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.07421
- [low/secondary_aggregator] **FrontierMath Tier 4 (research-level math) appears close to saturated in late 2026 according to third-party trackers. I could not confirm this on Epoch AI's own pages.**: Trackers report GPT-6.1 Sol 100%, GPT-6 Astra 97.6%, Claude Opus 5.5 95.0% on FrontierMath v2 Tier 4. The best open model is reportedly 46.3%. (2026-10). [BenchLM FrontierMath v2 Tier 4 leaderboard (aggregator)](https://benchlm.ai/benchmarks/frontiermathv2tier4). epoch.ai/frontiermath did not show scores when fetched. The 'Epoch declared Tier 4 saturated' claim (36kr) is unconfirmed. Verify at epoch.ai/benchmarks before publishing. FrontierMath has no head-to-head human baseline, so this measures difficulty, not 'vs humans'.
- [high/peer_reviewed] **GPQA (graduate-level 'Google-proof' science questions) human baselines: in-domain PhD experts scored about 65% (74% after discounting clear mistakes) on the broader set. Skilled non-experts with web access scored 34%. On the Diamond subset the expert figure is 81.3%, but it is inflated by how the subset was selected. OpenAI's recruited PhD experts scored 69.7% on Diamond.**: Experts 65% (Extended) / 81.3% (Diamond, selection-biased) / 69.7% (OpenAI's expert panel). Non-experts 34% / 22.1%. Top AI on Diamond: roughly 90-96%. (2023-11 (paper); AI scores 2025-2026). [Rein et al., GPQA (arXiv 2311.12022); Epoch AI GPQA Diamond page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12022). Epoch's own page lists Grok 4 at 87% (July 2025), and an Epoch post said top scores were ~95% (Opus 4.7 at 90%). 96% figures for 'GPT-6 Astra'/'Gemini 4 Argon' come from aggregators. Multiple-choice and likely saturated, with possible contamination. It is a test, not expert work.
- [high/preprint] **Microsoft's MAI-DxO orchestrator, using OpenAI o3, correctly diagnosed 85.5% of 304 sequential NEJM case-records. 21 practicing physicians scored 20% on the same cases.**: 85.5% at $7,184/case (MAI-DxO max config). 79.9% at $2,397. Physicians 20% at $2,963 (median 12 years' experience; US/UK). (2025-06/07 (arXiv 2506.22405)). [Nori et al., Sequential Diagnosis with Language Models (Microsoft AI)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.22405). Physicians were barred from textbooks, colleagues, internet or AI, which is far from real practice. The cases were rare, hard NEJM puzzles, not routine care. It is vendor-authored, not peer-reviewed at the time, and not a clinical trial. The 'higher accuracy AND lower cost' framing applies only to the ~80% configuration.
- [medium/peer_reviewed] **RCT (Pakistan, 60 physicians): the LLM alone outperformed physicians who had access to the LLM. Physicians with the LLM beat physicians using only conventional resources.**: LLM alone beat LLM-assisted physicians by 11.5 percentage points (95% CI 5.5-17.5). LLM-assisted physicians beat conventional-resources physicians by about 34 points on final diagnosis (preprint figure). (2026-02 (Nature Health)). [Large language model diagnostic assistance for physicians in a lower-middle-income country: a randomized controlled trial](https://www.nature.com/articles/s44360-025-00007-8). Figures come from search snippets; I couldn't fetch the full text because of a paywall/redirect. The cases were vignettes, not real patients. This pattern (AI alone > human+AI) has been reported several times since 2024.
- [high/peer_reviewed] **MASAI RCT (Sweden): AI-supported mammography screening was non-inferior to standard double reading by two radiologists for interval cancers. It detected more cancers and cut radiologist workload.**: Interval cancers 1.55 vs 1.76 per 1,000 (ratio 0.88, 95% CI 0.65-1.18; not significantly lower). Earlier analyses: 29% higher cancer detection with no rise in false positives, and 44% less screen-reading workload. N=105,934 women. (2026-01 (The Lancet)). [MASAI interval cancer results (via ASCO Post / Lancet)](https://ascopost.com/news/february-2026/randomized-trial-shows-ai-supported-mammography-improves-sensitivity-and-lowers-interval-cancer-rate/). This is AI + one radiologist vs two radiologists, not AI alone vs humans. The '12% fewer interval cancers' headline is from the vendor's (ScreenPoint) press release; use the per-1,000 numbers.
- [high/primary] **ForecastBench (Forecasting Research Institute): the best AI forecasters are now statistically indistinguishable from superforecasters. For the first time, an AI ranked above the superforecaster median on market questions.**: Top system Cassi AI, p=0.41 (one-sided bootstrap vs equal accuracy). xAI p=0.15-0.16, Google DeepMind p=0.14. In January 2026, superforecasters still led by 0.017 Brier points. (2026-07-16). [FRI Substack: AI models have likely reached parity with superforecasters on ForecastBench](https://forecastingresearch.substack.com/p/ai-models-have-likely-reached-parity). PARITY, NOT SUPERHUMAN. The superforecaster baseline was elicited in 2024 and is extrapolated. Good Judgment argues the setup favors AI. A fresh superforecaster rematch is scheduled for fall 2026; I found no results yet.
- [high/peer_reviewed] **DeepMind's GenCast (diffusion-based ensemble weather model) was more skillful than ECMWF's ENS, the leading operational ensemble, on most verification targets.**: Better than ENS on 97.2% of 1,320 targets (99.8% at lead times >36h). 15-day ensemble in ~8 minutes on one TPU v5. (2024-12 (Nature)). [Price et al., Probabilistic weather forecasting with machine learning (GenCast), Nature 2024; arXiv 2312.15796](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.15796). ECMWF noted the comparison used an older, coarser ENS. Comparisons are against physics models, not human forecasters.
- [medium/peer_reviewed] **In the 2025 Atlantic season, Google DeepMind's AI cyclone model had lower track errors than the official NHC forecast and all major physics models. In 2026 NHC openly leaned on it.**: 72-h Atlantic track error: GDMI 66.5 nmi vs NHC official 81.4, ECMWF 102.7, GFS 128.6 (James Franklin's analysis as reported). Nature paper: 5-day ensemble-mean track error 230 km vs 370 km ENS. (2025 season; Nature paper 2026; CNN 2026-10-08). [NPR (2025-11-29); Nature 'Operational tropical cyclone forecasting with AI'; CNN 'The National Hurricane Center's big bet on AI'](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-026-10953-2). The 72-h numbers came via a secondary source (Horizon Search) citing Franklin; check NPR/Franklin directly. The 2026 comparisons are preliminary until NHC's verification report (spring 2027). This is a case where AI beats an expert human team's official product.
- [medium/vendor_reported] **Waymo's fully driverless fleet is involved in far fewer injury crashes than a location-matched human-driver benchmark.**: Over 270M rider-only miles through June 2026: 95% fewer serious-injury-or-worse crashes, 82% fewer injury crashes, 93% fewer pedestrian-injury crashes, 86% fewer cyclist-injury crashes. (2026-09-24 (data through 2026-06)). [Waymo: Safety data update, September 2026](https://waymo.com/blog/shorts/safetydata-september26/). Waymo's own analysis, with no confidence intervals on the page. The comparison is with average human drivers, not expert drivers. Peer-reviewed analysis of 56.7M miles (Traffic Injury Prevention, 2025) found similar significant reductions. There was a 2026 freeway pause/recall that I did not investigate.
- [high/peer_reviewed] **In controlled online debates, GPT-4 given basic personal data about its opponent was more persuasive than human debaters.**: In non-tied pairs, the personalized GPT-4 was more persuasive 64.4% of the time (81.2% relative increase in odds of higher post-debate agreement; 95% CI +26.0% to +160.7%). ~900 participants. (2025-05 (Nature Human Behaviour)). [Salvi, Horta Ribeiro, Gallotti, West: On the conversational persuasiveness of GPT-4](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-025-02194-6). Human opponents were ordinary crowdworkers, not expert persuaders. Media often misreport the 81% figure as '81% more likely to change minds'. Separately, Hackenburg et al. (Science, Dec 2025; N=76,977, 19 LLMs) found post-training/prompting raised persuasiveness up to 51%/27% but reduced factual accuracy. That study had no human-persuader comparison.
- [high/official_leaderboard] **On Scale AI's Remote Labor Index (real paid freelance projects), even the best 2026 agents complete only about a fifth of projects at acceptable client quality.**: Automation rate: Opus 5.5 21.25%, GPT-6 Astra 20.83%, Fable 5.1 17.92%, Gemini Argon 4 17.08%. At launch (Oct 2025) the best was Manus at 2.5%. The 240 projects had paid humans $143,991 in total. (Leaderboard fetched 2026-11 (undated page); paper 2025-10). [Scale AI / CAIS Remote Labor Index leaderboard; arXiv 2510.26787](https://labs.scale.com/leaderboard/rli). Key jagged-frontier evidence: ~80% of real remote jobs still fail. It rose ~8x in a year, which supports 'rapid progress' but not 'superhuman at work'.
- [medium/official_leaderboard] **ARC-AGI-3 (interactive game-like reasoning environments, calibrated so humans can solve every one) launched with frontier LLMs near zero. Scores rose sharply by autumn 2026.**: Launch (2026-03-25): Opus 4.6 0.50%, Gemini 3.1 Pro 0.40%, GPT-5.4 0.20%; humans solve 100% of environments. ARC Prize verified Claude Opus 5 at 30.16% (2026-07-24). arcprize.org/leaderboard 'Standard' column: GPT-6 Astra 62.7%, GPT-6.1 Sol 52.7%, Gemini 4 Argon 49.8%. arcprize.org/results lists GPT-6 99.95%, Gemini 4 Argon 97.46%, GPT-6.1 Sol 96.37%. (2026-03 to 2026-10). [ARC Prize leaderboard and results pages; ARC-AGI-3 technical report](https://arcprize.org/leaderboard). CONFLICT: the two official ARC Prize pages show very different numbers for the same models. The leaderboard shows a separate 'Provider adapter' column (e.g. GPT-6 Astra 99.9%), so the ~97-99% figures likely use provider-specific harnesses. Costs ran $5.8K-$26K, units unclear. ARC defines 100% as 'beat every game as efficiently as humans'; I could not find the exact action-efficiency formula or the human reference player. Do not claim AI is superhuman at action efficiency without that.
- [high/primary] **Robots remain far below humans at general physical tasks. They work reliably only in narrow warehouse or inspection settings.**: Robots are typically 3-10x slower than humans (2-10x on household tasks). No robot completed all kitchen-cleaning subtasks or removed a trash bag from an indoor bin. Key-turning ~52% reliability at ~5x slower. Furniture assembly and cooking are at the early research stage. Generalizing to new objects or environments is the main bottleneck. (2026-02-10). [Epoch AI: Where Autonomy Works — Evaluating Robot Capabilities in 2026](https://epoch.ai/publications/where-autonomy-works-evaluating-robot-capabilities-in-2026). Coverage of the Stanford AI Index 2026 claims humanoids fully and safely complete only ~12% of real household tasks; I did not verify this at hai.stanford.edu. Figure claims chore success in new homes went from 9% to 56% (company-reported, 3 behaviors).
- [medium/peer_reviewed] **Google DeepMind's Co-Scientist (multi-agent hypothesis generator) proposed drug-repurposing candidates for acute myeloid leukaemia, and some were validated in vitro.**: 30 top-ranked hypotheses went to oncologists, 5 were lab-tested, and 1 was particularly promising, with efficacy at clinically relevant concentrations. Published alongside FutureHouse's Robin (ripasudil proposed for dry AMD). (2026-05 (Nature 655, 487-496)). [Accelerating scientific discovery with Co-Scientist (Nature)](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-026-10644-y). Humans chose which candidates to test, so this is AI-assisted discovery, not autonomous. It has no clinical validation and no comparison with conventional computational methods. Citation details come from search snippets; check the article page.
- [high/peer_reviewed] **Game-playing: AI has been decisively superhuman for years in Go, chess, shogi, no-limit poker (6-player), Gran Turismo racing, and human-level or better in Diplomacy.**: AlphaGo beat Lee Sedol 4-1 (2016). AlphaZero/Stockfish-class engines are far above the best human Elo. Pluribus beat elite pros at 6-max no-limit hold'em (Science, 2019). GT Sophy beat top Gran Turismo drivers (Nature, 2022). Cicero placed in the top 10% of human Diplomacy players (Science, 2022). (2016-2022). [Brown & Sandholm, Superhuman AI for multiplayer poker, Science 2019 (and the respective Nature/Science papers)](https://www.science.org/doi/10.1126/science.aay2400). Not re-checked by search this session (search budget exhausted). These are well-established historical results. Note: in 2023 amateur humans beat superhuman Go AIs (KataGo) using adversarial strategies, so 'superhuman' can still be brittle.
- [medium/official_leaderboard] **Humanity's Last Exam: the official lastexam.ai table still shows older figures, while 2026 aggregator figures conflict widely.**: Official page: Gemini 3 Pro 38.3%, GPT-5 25.3% (dataset updated 2025-04-03). Aggregators (Oct-Nov 2026) list leaders between 54.8% (GPT-6 Astra) and 70% (Kimi K3.1 Pro), depending on tools and setup. (2025-2026). [Humanity's Last Exam official site](https://lastexam.ai). HLE publishes no human expert baseline, so it cannot support a superhuman claim either way. The official page appears stale. HLE-Diamond was released 2026-09-22.
- [medium/peer_reviewed] **GDPval (expert-graded professional deliverables across 44 occupations): frontier models approach expert quality, but reported numbers conflict.**: ICLR 2026 paper: Claude Opus 4.1 top at 47.6% wins-or-ties vs industry professionals on the gold subset. OpenAI later reported GPT-5.2 Thinking at 70.9%. A blog reports ~49-51% for GPT-5.6 Sol / Claude Opus 5 (unverified). (2025-09 to 2026-08). [GDPval (ICLR 2026)](https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2026/file/290c2430f91912204f30bbcc990fff1d-Paper-Conference.pdf). 'Win or tie' at ~50% means parity with experts on well-specified one-shot tasks, not superhuman. The numbers are hard to reconcile (different task subsets or graders). The 70.9% is vendor-reported.

### surprises

- In July 2026 two Chinese industry models (Huawei's Celia and Xiaohongshu/RedNote's dots-note-3.0), not OpenAI or Google, were the ones announcing officially graded perfect IMO scores. Only 7 of 666 human teenagers matched them.
- At the 2025 ICPC World Finals, AI solved a problem (Problem C) that none of the 139 elite university teams could solve.
- An AI model disproved an 80-year-old Erdős geometry conjecture (unit distances, 1946). Noga Alon says he has stopped working on Erdős problems because AI now solves them.
- In a 2026 randomized trial, doctors given an AI assistant still did worse than the AI working alone, by 11.5 percentage points.
- The US National Hurricane Center in 2026 shaped official forecasts to closely match Google DeepMind's AI model, which had lower track errors than NHC's own official forecasts in 2025.
- Waymo reports 95% fewer serious-injury crashes than human drivers over 270M+ driverless miles.
- Despite all this, the best AI agents fail about 4 out of 5 real freelance jobs, and no robot can yet reliably take out the trash from an indoor bin.
- ARC-AGI-3 went from under 1% for every frontier model in March 2026 to tens of percent (or ~97-99% with special harnesses) by October 2026, one of the fastest rises recorded on any benchmark.

### counterevidence

- Real work: the best agents complete only ~21% of real freelance projects on Scale's Remote Labor Index (Nov 2026), even as they ace olympiads. Benchmark superhumanity does not equal job superhumanity.
- Robotics and physical tasks remain clearly sub-human. Robots are 3-10x slower, fail at multi-step household tasks (kitchen cleaning, trash removal, furniture assembly, cooking), and do not generalize to new environments (Epoch AI, Feb 2026).
- ARC-AGI-3 debuted in March 2026 with frontier LLMs at 0.2-0.5% vs humans at 100%. That shows novel interactive learning was a large gap only eight months ago. The late-2026 'near 100%' figures appear to depend on provider-specific harnesses, and the official ARC Prize pages disagree with each other.
- Forecasting is parity, not superhuman. FRI's p-values (0.14-0.41) cannot reject equal accuracy, and the superforecaster baseline dates from 2024.
- Medicine: the headline diagnostic studies (MAI-DxO 85.5% vs 20%) use rare NEJM puzzle cases with physicians denied normal resources. RCTs mostly show AI as an aid (MASAI: non-inferior on interval cancers, not significantly better). Patient outcomes are not yet shown to improve.
- In 2025, AI IMO gold (35/42) was below the best humans: five humans scored perfect. IOI 2025: five humans still beat OpenAI. Only in 2026 did AI match the very top at IMO (tied with 7 humans at 42/42, not exceeding them, since 42 is the maximum).
- Verification problems: OpenAI's October 2025 claim that GPT-5 solved 10 Erdős problems was wrong (literature rediscovery) and was retracted. Some 2026 'AI solutions' were also rediscoveries. Mathematicians warn that many long AI proofs go unchecked.
- Persuasion: the 'superhuman persuasion' result is against ordinary crowdworkers in short online debates. The large Science 2025 study found that persuasion gains came with lower factual accuracy, and real-world impact may be limited.
- Superhuman Go AIs were beaten by amateur humans using adversarial strategies (2023), so narrow superhumanity can be brittle.
- Multiple-choice expert benchmarks (GPQA) are saturated and possibly contaminated. Beating PhDs on a 198-question test is not the same as doing a PhD's work.
- Stanford AI Index 2026 coverage repeatedly cites a 'can win math gold but can't tell time' finding (analog clock reading) and a gap between benchmark performance and real-world reliability. I could not verify the exact clock-reading numbers (search budget exhausted).

Domains: IMO, ICPC, GPQA, weather, hurricane track, Waymo, MAI-DxO, forecasting (parity), GDPval (~parity), RLI (far below), robotics (far below), ARC-AGI-3 (contested).

### open_questions

- IMO 2026: is there an official IMO (imo-official.org) statement confirming which AI entries were officially graded? Did Google DeepMind or OpenAI officially enter in 2026? The AFP report did not say, and my search budget ran out before I could check.
- ARC-AGI-3: what are the exact action-efficiency scoring formula and the human reference baseline? Which ARC Prize number is the headline: the leaderboard 'Standard' column (~50-63%) or the results page (~96-99.95%)? The two official ARC Prize pages conflict.
- FrontierMath Tier 4: do Epoch AI's own pages confirm the ~95-100% scores and the 'saturated' claim? Aggregators report this, but epoch.ai did not show scores when fetched.
- Humanity's Last Exam: the official lastexam.ai leaderboard appears stale (Gemini 3 Pro 38.3%), while aggregators report 55-70% for 2026 models. What is the authoritative current figure?
- Codeforces: I found no verified 2026 source showing AI surpassing the top human ratings. Aggregator self-reports cite ~3,450-3,470 for 2026 models, but these are vendor-reported and not comparable across labs.
- IOI 2026: I could not confirm any official AI-track result. The NVIDIA Nemotron 'gold' claim appears only in secondary press.
- ForecastBench fall-2026 rematch with fresh superforecaster forecasts: results not yet found.
- Translation: I found no clean 2025-2026 human-evaluation study showing LLMs beat professional human translators overall (WMT25 findings paper not fully read). Treat 'superhuman translation' as unverified.
- Protein structure/design: I did not re-verify AlphaFold/CASP figures or any 2026 protein-design result this session (search budget exhausted).
- The 2026 model names (GPT-6 Astra, GPT-6.1 Sol, Gemini 4 Argon, Claude Opus 5/5.5, Fable 5) appear on official leaderboards (ARC Prize, Scale RLI), but I did not fetch vendor launch posts. Verify names and dates before publishing.
- GDPval: the 47.6% (paper), 70.9% (OpenAI, GPT-5.2) and ~50% (2026 blog) figures conflict. Which task subset and grader produced each?
- The Stanford AI Index 2026 'can't tell time' clock-reading result and the '12% household task success' humanoid figure came only through secondary coverage and need checking against hai.stanford.edu.
- The search budget (200 WebSearch calls per turn, shared across agents) was exhausted before full verification; a follow-up run is needed for the items above.

## perception

The evidence shows a measurable, two-sided perception gap. On the 'people underestimate AI' side: forecasters have repeatedly been too conservative. FRI's Sept 2026 accuracy review finds that in 2022, superforecasters gave the outcomes that actually happened on MATH, MMLU, QuALITY and IMO gold an average probability of 9.7%, and domain experts gave 24.6%. Experts expected IMO gold around 2030 and superforecasters around 2035; it happened in July 2025. LEAP panelists expected 14% on LiveCodeBench Pro Hard by end-2026, and the actual score was 53.8% by May 2026. Revenue forecasts were too low by 2-5x. The public remains skeptical of current ability. In Bentley-Gallup (May 2026), Americans still rate AI worse than people on every task asked about. In Economist/YouGov (May-June 2026), 43% say AI reasons worse than humans and 17% say better; a July 2026 YouGov poll was roughly evenly split, 33% better vs 29% worse. Most people use free tiers. Reuters Institute (Oct 2026, 9 countries) finds about 18% have any paid access and that free-only users use agents far less: 11% vs 39%. OpenAI's own figures (50M paying subscribers vs 900M weekly users in Feb 2026) imply about 5-6% pay. Anthropic's Oct 2026 survey (its own staff vs the US public, so not neutral) shows an extreme insider-outsider split: 91% vs 39% expect Nobel-level discovery by 2030. Public mood is souring even as use grows: Pew finds 52% more concerned than excited (June 2026), and Gallup finds 39% say AI does more harm than good, up from 31%. The counterevidence is real and should get equal space. METR's 2025 RCT found experienced developers were 19% slower with AI while believing they were 20% faster. Its 2026 follow-up gave -18% and -4% time changes, with confidence intervals crossing zero, and METR itself calls the data unreliable. The Danish administrative-data study rules out earnings or hours effects larger than 2%. On the Remote Labor Index, the best agent was judged at least as good as a human on only 2.5% of freelance projects at launch, and the best is about 21% now. Forecasters also overestimated some things: SWE-Bench Verified in 2025, LLM biorisk uplift (predicted 16-40% vs actual 5.2%), robotaxi share, and Hinton's 2016 radiologist prediction. AI-washing is now a recurring enforcement category (SEC: Delphia, Global Predictions, Presto; SEC and DOJ: Nate). For the author's beliefs: belief 1 (the trend is faster than expected) is well supported on benchmarks and revenue. Belief 2 (more superhuman than people realize) is supported as a perception gap, but measured real-world productivity effects are still small or uncertain. FRI also warns that its method is biased toward detecting underestimation.

### Fact-check verdict

The core numbers check out. Every figure taken from FRI, AI Digest, METR (2025 and 2026), the Remote Labor Index, Anthropic, Bentley-Gallup and both YouGov polls matches its primary source exactly, and nothing looks fabricated. The real problems are framing and completeness. (1) The revenue 'misses' rest on FRI's 'likely' current values, built from press reports of vendor run rates (Anthropic $100B via Axios, OpenAI $40B via Bloomberg), and the questions resolve at end-2026; the omitted superforecaster forecast of $90B shrinks the combined-run-rate gap to about 1.6x. (2) The Danish study has been revised and retitled. Its 2% bound is measured from ChatGPT's launch, not from adoption, and the researcher's note that '1% is a misstatement' is probably wrong, since 1% was likely the original version's bound. Sample sizes and the 3% time saving went unverified. (3) The summary lists robotaxi share as an overestimate, but it is unresolved and only the experts were high. Hinton's prediction has no source. (4) The RLI top two differ by one project and have no CI. (5) The Bentley-Gallup 39% harm figure equals the 2023 level (40%), so it is a rebound, not a record. The search budget ran out after one search, so the Reuters Institute, OpenAI subscriber, Pew and most SEC/DOJ claims in the summary still need checking before publication. The overall thesis (benchmarks and revenue beat forecasts; productivity effects small or uncertain; FRI's method is biased toward finding underestimation) is fairly supported by the verified sources.

- **confirmed**: XPT 2022: average probability assigned to the observed outcomes on MATH, MMLU, QuALITY and IMO Gold was 9.7% for superforecasters and 24.6% for domain experts → Superforecasters 9.7%, domain experts 24.6%. These are average implied probabilities for the observed resolution values on the four XPT benchmarks (forecasts made mid-2022). FRI published on 2026-09-22. (https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). Matches the primary source. FRI says outright that its methodology is biased toward finding underestimates, and that it analyses medians, which can hide more accurate subsets. The page does not say explicitly that all four questions have formally resolved.
- **confirmed**: IMO gold: expert median forecast 2030, superforecasters 2035, achieved July 2025; implied probability of the outcome 8.6% (experts) and 2.3% (superforecasters) → Expert median 2030, superforecaster median 2035, achieved July 2025. Implied probabilities 8.6% (experts) and 2.3% (superforecasters), per FRI footnote 5. (https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). Exact match. The July 2025 gold-level results came from the labs themselves (OpenAI and Google DeepMind), so the resolution event is vendor-announced. That is widely accepted, but worth noting.
- **confirmed**: LEAP: FrontierMath T1-3 end-2025 forecast 31% (experts) / 30% (superforecasters) vs 40.7% actual; LiveCodeBench Pro Hard end-2026 forecast 14% / 12% vs 53.8% SOTA by May 2026; open-weight 20% vs 22.7% → FrontierMath: 31% / 30% forecast vs 40.7% (unadjusted). LiveCodeBench Pro Hard: 14% / 12% forecast vs 53.8% SOTA in May 2026. Open-weight question: 20% forecast median vs 22.7% resolution. (https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). Matches. Two caveats. LiveCodeBench Pro resolves at end-2026, so it is not formally resolved, although the forecast is already exceeded. The 40.7% FrontierMath score predates Epoch's finding that about 42% of problems contained errors. Note also that FrontierMath is funded by OpenAI, which has privileged access to it.
- **confirmed**: METR 80% time horizon end-2026 forecast 3.4 h (experts) / 3.5 h (superforecasters); Claude Mythos Preview about 3.1 h by May 8, 2026 → 80% horizon (not 50%). Experts 3.4 h, superforecasters 3.5 h. Mythos Preview about 3 h 6 min, added May 8, 2026. (https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). Correct as of FRI's Sept 2026 writeup. METR's time-horizons page still lists May 8, 2026 as its latest model addition, so I could not get a newer measurement. The briefing should say the figure is 'as of May 2026' and that this question is still unresolved.
- **corrected**: Revenue/adoption: AI-company ARR end-2026 forecast $20B (experts) / $25B (superforecasters) vs about $100B; Anthropic+OpenAI run rate forecast $70B vs about $140B; US gen-AI-assisted work hours 2025 forecast 4% vs 5.7% → ARR: experts $20B, economists $16B, superforecasters $25B; FRI's 'likely' current value is $100B. Combined Anthropic+OpenAI run rate: experts $70B, superforecasters $90B; 'likely' value $140B (OpenAI $40B per Bloomberg, Aug 13, 2026 + Anthropic $100B per Axios, Sept 18, 2026). Work hours: experts 4%, superforecasters 3.6%, resolved 5.7%. The question's 2% baseline was later revised to 3.35%. (https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). The numbers are right but incomplete. The superforecaster $90B figure and the economists' $16B are omitted. FRI calls the revenue values 'likely', not resolved, and they come from press reports of vendor run rates, so they are vendor-reported. The $100B 'AI-focused company' value is sourced only to an Axios report on Anthropic's revenue. On work hours, part of the miss is probably forecasters anchoring on a baseline that was later revised up.
- **confirmed**: AI Digest 2025 survey (421 forecasters): SWE-Bench Verified 88% forecast vs 80.9%; Cybench 62% vs 82%; OSWorld 60% vs 66.3%; FrontierMath 40% vs 40.7%; frontier-lab revenue $16B vs $30.4B; 'most important problem' 2% vs 0.625% → All values match. Published Jan 16, 2026 by Anson Ho (Epoch AI), with the AI Futures Project. Revenue covers OpenAI, Anthropic and xAI. (https://theaidigest.org/2025-forecast-results). The sample is self-selected (73% claim AI experience; half expect HLMI by 2030), so it is not a neutral forecaster pool. RE-Bench was also close (1.1 forecast vs 1.13 actual). The revenue question had the lowest 80% CI coverage (57.7%).
- **confirmed**: Biorisk uplift: biosecurity experts 22.5%, virologists 40%, superforecasters 16.2% predicted vs 5.2% actual → 22.5% / 40% / 16.2% predicted vs 5.2% actual (mid-2025 RCT, STEM undergrads completing all three tasks). (https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). Exact match. The summary's range '16-40%' is correct.
- **confirmed**: METR 2025 RCT: 16 developers, 246 tasks, 19% slower (95% CI +2% to +39%); forecast 24% speedup, believed 20% afterward → 16 developers, 246 issues, 19% longer with AI, 24% expected speedup, 20% believed speedup afterward. Tools were mainly Cursor Pro with Claude 3.5/3.7 Sonnet. The page is marked 'out of date'. (https://metr.org/blog/2025-07-10-early-2025-ai-experienced-os-dev-study/). The +2% to +39% CI is not on the 2025 page, which says clustered SEs are forthcoming. It does appear in METR's Feb 24, 2026 post, as the researcher noted.
- **confirmed**: METR late-2025 follow-up: 57 developers (10 returning, 47 new), 143 repos, 800+ tasks, $50/h; returning -18% (CI -38% to +9%), new -4% (CI -15% to +9%); 30-50% withheld tasks; METR calls the signal unreliable → All figures match the Feb 24, 2026 post. METR calls the data 'an unreliable signal' and 'only very weak evidence for the size of this increase'. (https://metr.org/blog/2026-02-24-uplift-update/). Correct, and correctly framed. On the self-report survey the researcher flagged: the page instead features a separate METR survey of 349 technical workers with a median self-reported 1.4-2x change in value of work. The '3x' figure is not on METR's page, so it should stay out.
- **corrected**: Danish admin data (Humlum & Vestergaard, NBER WP 33777): null earnings/hours effects; CIs rule out effects >2% two years after adoption; about 3% time savings; about 25,000 workers, 7,000 workplaces, 11 occupations → NBER w33777 (issued May 2025, revised March 2026) is now titled 'Still Waters, Rapid Currents: Early Labor Market Transformation under Generative AI'. It reports precise null effects on earnings and recorded hours and rules out effects 'larger than 2% two years after the launch of ChatGPT'. (https://www.nber.org/papers/w33777). Four problems. (1) The bound is defined relative to ChatGPT's launch, not 'after adoption'. (2) The paper has been retitled and revised, so the briefing should cite the current version. (3) The original 2025 'Small Labor Market Effects' abstract, from memory, stated a 1% bound, so 'secondary sources misstate it as 1%' is probably wrong: 1% was likely the earlier version's figure. (4) I could not verify the sample sizes or the 3% time-savings figure: the BFI PDF did not extract and the NBER abstract omits them. The revised abstract also adds findings on task reorganisation and occupational mobility that the researcher left out.
- **confirmed**: Remote Labor Index: best agent 2.5% at launch; current top Opus 5.5 21.25%, GPT 6 Astra 20.83%, Fable 5.1 17.92% → Top three: Opus 5.5 21.25%, GPT 6 Astra 20.83%, Fable 5.1 17.92%. Gemini Argon 4 (marked NEW) 17.08%. Manus was top at 2.5% at launch. (https://labs.scale.com/leaderboard/rli). Matches. The leaderboard states there is no CI for Automation Rate and gives no snapshot date. With about 240 projects, a step of about 0.42 pp is one project, so the top two (21.25 vs 20.83) differ by a single project. Do not rank them. Scale AI is a commercial data vendor and co-runs the benchmark.
- **confirmed**: Anthropic expectations gap: 91% staff vs 39% public expect Nobel-level discovery by 2030; never-able at most 2% vs 19-40%; own work by 2030 75% vs 28%; GDP 2030 17.1% vs 5.4% → All numbers match. Published Oct 16, 2026. Public sample: Morning Consult opt-in panel, N=10,980, Aug 11-23, 2026, weighted. Staff: N=1,334, Aug 9-12, 2026, unweighted. Re-field Sept 17-24 (N=7,613): Nobel 44%, GDP 5.6%. (https://www.anthropic.com/research/ai-expectations-gap). Vendor-produced, correctly labeled. Further caveats: the 28% is US workers (employed respondents), not all adults. Only half the public sample was asked about 2030. Answer formats differed (public fixed ranges vs staff slider), so the comparisons are not like for like. The re-field moved the Nobel figure 5 points in a month, which suggests instability.
- **confirmed**: Bentley-Gallup: AI rated worse than people on every task (2023 and 2026); 'worse' down 7/6/5 pts (driving, financial, medical); homework 'AI better' 26% to 29%; harm 39% vs 31% (2025); good 9% → Matches. Published July 28, 2026; fielded May 4-11, 2026; N=3,270 Gallup Panel; MOE ±2.4. 'Equal harm and good' is 52%. 'More harm than good' was 40% in 2023. (https://news.gallup.com/poll/712751/americans-cool-toward.aspx). Add context: harm was 40% in 2023, so 39% is a rebound to the 2023 level, not a new high. The article does not state the baseline year for the point changes, though 2023 is implied. Hiring recommendations were essentially unchanged.
- **confirmed**: Economist/YouGov May 29-Jun 1, 2026: AI reasoning worse 43%, same 21%, better 17%. YouGov Jul 16-20, 2026 (N=1,110): better 33%, worse 29%, same 25%; facts 44/18; complex reasoning 33/28; creativity 25/37 → All figures match. The July poll was published Aug 1, 2026 (N=1,110 US adult citizens, MOE about ±4.0). The Economist poll item covers AI's 'thinking and reasoning abilities' compared with humans; N is not given in the article. (https://yougov.com/en-us/articles/55289-do-americans-think-ai-is-smarter-than-donald-trump). The researcher is right that the two polls use different wording (humans in general vs 'the average person'), so the summary should not imply a shift from 43% worse to 29% worse. About 19% of the Economist sample did not answer or was unsure.

### Missed evidence (from the fact-checker)

- Verification limits: the shared web-search budget ran out after one search. Everything above was checked by fetching known primary URLs directly. Several summary-level claims could not be re-verified and should be confirmed before publication: Reuters Institute Oct 2026 (18% paid access; agent use 11% vs 39%), OpenAI's Feb 2026 figures (50M paying vs 900M weekly users), Pew June 2026 (52% more concerned than excited), and the SEC/DOJ Presto and Nate actions. Of the SEC claims, only the Delphia/Global Predictions release (2024-36, Mar 18, 2024, $400K total penalties) was confirmed.
- FRI lists additional overestimates the summary omits. XPT software/information-services GDP share: forecast 3.6% (experts) / 3.45% (superforecasters) vs 3.25% actual. US electricity share for AI and AI-companionship use (2027) are possible overestimates. https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update
- The robotaxi share is an unresolved 2027 question: expert median 7.3% vs an LLM projection of 2.5% and a superforecaster median of 2%. FRI says the expert figure 'looks very likely' to be an overestimate, so the summary should not present it as a resolved miss. Superforecasters were roughly on target here, so 'forecasters overestimated' applies to experts only. https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update
- Hinton's 2016 radiologist prediction is not in the FRI source and has no citation in the findings. Add a primary source, such as the 2016 talk video, or drop it.
- FRI's ARR values come from press reports of vendor run rates (Axios on Anthropic, Bloomberg on OpenAI). The headline 'revenue forecasts were too low by 2-5x' therefore compares forecasts with unaudited vendor figures, not resolved values.
- NBER w33777 has been revised (March 2026) and retitled 'Still Waters, Rapid Currents'. The revised abstract adds evidence of task reorganisation and occupational upgrading among adopters. Cite the current version: https://www.nber.org/papers/w33777
- METR has a separate survey of 349 technical workers reporting a median self-reported 1.4-2x change in the value of their work. That is better primary evidence for the self-report side of the perception gap than the unverified '3x' figure from secondary blogs. https://metr.org/blog/2026-02-24-uplift-update/
- AI Digest's forecaster pool was self-selected and AI-optimistic: half expect HLMI by 2030. Revenue had the worst calibration (57.7% of 80% CIs contained the true value), which strengthens the underestimation-on-revenue point. https://theaidigest.org/2025-forecast-results

### Findings

- [high/primary] **Forecasters in the 2022 Existential Risk Persuasion Tournament (XPT) gave low probabilities to the benchmark outcomes that actually occurred**: Average probability assigned to observed outcomes on MATH, MMLU, QuALITY and IMO Gold: superforecasters 9.7%, domain experts 24.6% (2026-09-22 (forecasts made mid-2022)). [How Accurate Have AI Progress Forecasts Been So Far? (Forecasting Research Institute)](https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). FRI itself notes that its method is biased toward finding underestimation, because overestimates can only be confirmed after resolution dates pass.
- [high/primary] **IMO gold-level performance arrived years ahead of the median forecasts**: Experts' median forecast: 2030. Superforecasters': 2035. Achieved July 2025. Implied probability of the actual outcome: 8.6% (experts), 2.3% (superforecasters) (2026-09-22). [How Accurate Have AI Progress Forecasts Been So Far? (FRI)](https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). XPT forecasts were made in 2022.
- [high/primary] **LEAP panel forecasts on frontier coding and math benchmarks were too low**: FrontierMath Tiers 1-3, end of 2025: experts forecast 31%, superforecasters 30%, actual 40.7%. LiveCodeBench Pro (Hard), end of 2026: experts 14%, superforecasters 12%, actual state of the art 53.8% by May 2026 (2026-09-22). [How Accurate Have AI Progress Forecasts Been So Far? (FRI)](https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). The FrontierMath score is unadjusted; FRI notes that Epoch later found errors in about 42% of problems. Forecasts for open-weight models were close (20% forecast vs 22.7% actual).
- [medium/primary] **The LEAP forecast for the METR time horizon is not yet clearly beaten**: METR 80%-success time horizon, end of 2026: experts forecast 3.4 h, superforecasters 3.5 h. Claude Mythos Preview reached about 3.1 h by May 8, 2026 (2026-09-22). [How Accurate Have AI Progress Forecasts Been So Far? (FRI)](https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). Still on track and unresolved, not a clear miss. Useful as a check against claims that every forecast is blown past.
- [medium/primary] **Revenue and adoption forecasts were far too low**: Revenue of AI-focused companies (ARR), end of 2026: experts forecast $20B, superforecasters $25B; current value cited about $100B. Anthropic + OpenAI combined run rate: experts forecast $70B; about $140B cited. US work hours assisted by generative AI in 2025: experts forecast 4%; actual 5.7% (2026-09-22). [How Accurate Have AI Progress Forecasts Been So Far? (FRI)](https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). The 'current' revenue values are run rates reported by companies and relayed by FRI, i.e. vendor-reported.
- [high/primary] **In AI Digest's 2025 forecasting survey, forecasters were roughly right on most benchmarks but low on revenue, and high on SWE-Bench**: 421 forecasters. SWE-Bench Verified: forecast 88%, actual 80.9% (too high). Cybench: forecast 62%, actual 82%. OSWorld: forecast 60%, actual 66.3%. FrontierMath: forecast 40%, actual 40.7%. Frontier-lab annualized revenue: forecast $16B, actual $30.4B (2026-01-16). [How well did forecasters predict 2025 AI progress? (AI Digest / Epoch AI, Anson Ho)](https://theaidigest.org/2025-forecast-results). Mixed picture: not uniformly too conservative. Forecasters also overestimated public concern about AI as the 'most important problem' (forecast 2%, actual 0.625%).
- [high/primary] **Expert forecasts for AI biorisk uplift were too high**: Predicted share of STEM undergrads completing 3 biorisk tasks with LLM help: biosecurity experts 22.5%, virologists 40%, superforecasters 16.2%. Actual: 5.2% (2026-09-22 (RCT mid-2025)). [How Accurate Have AI Progress Forecasts Been So Far? (FRI)](https://forecastingresearch.org/research/ai-progress-accuracy-update). Counterevidence: a case where experts overestimated what AI enables in the real world.
- [high/primary] **METR's 2025 randomized trial found AI slowed experienced developers while they believed it sped them up**: 16 developers, 246 tasks. Tasks took 19% longer with AI allowed (95% CI +2% to +39%). Developers forecast a 24% speedup beforehand and still believed in a 20% speedup afterward (2025-07-10). [Measuring the Impact of Early-2025 AI on Experienced Open-Source Developer Productivity (METR)](https://metr.org/blog/2025-07-10-early-2025-ai-experienced-os-dev-study/). Tools were mainly Cursor Pro with Claude 3.5/3.7 Sonnet. METR now marks the page as out of date. The CI is from METR's Feb 2026 post.
- [high/primary] **METR's late-2025 follow-up points toward speedup, but METR says it is unreliable**: 57 developers (10 returning, 47 new), 143 repositories, 800+ tasks, pay $50/h (was $150/h). Time change with AI: returning developers -18% (CI -38% to +9%); new developers -4% (CI -15% to +9%). 30-50% of developers withheld tasks they did not want to do without AI (2026-02-24). [We are Changing our Developer Productivity Experiment Design (METR)](https://metr.org/blog/2026-02-24-uplift-update/). METR says the new data 'gives us an unreliable signal' and is 'only very weak evidence' for the size of the increase. Selection bias likely pushes the speedup estimate down. A METR self-report survey with a 3x median speedup appears only in secondary blogs; not verified.
- [high/preprint] **Danish administrative data show no detectable earnings or hours effects from AI chatbots**: Null effects on earnings and recorded hours. CIs rule out effects larger than 2% two years after adoption. Average time savings about 3%. About 25,000 workers, 7,000 workplaces, 11 occupations (2025-05 (NBER WP 33777)). [Large Language Models, Small Labor Market Effects (Humlum & Vestergaard)](https://bfi.uchicago.edu/wp-content/uploads/2025/04/BFI_WP_2025-56-3.pdf). Data cover 2023-24, so models are older. Some secondary sources misstate the bound as 1%.
- [medium/official_leaderboard] **AI agents can complete only a minority of real paid freelance projects at human-acceptable quality**: Automation rate (share of projects where the AI deliverable is judged at least as good as the human one): best agent 2.5% at launch (Oct 2025). Current leaderboard top: Opus 5.5 at 21.25%, GPT 6 Astra 20.83%, Fable 5.1 17.92% (2025-10 launch; leaderboard read 2026-11-07). [Remote Labor Index (Scale AI / Center for AI Safety) leaderboard](https://labs.scale.com/leaderboard/rli). The leaderboard shows no CI and no update date. The paper is arXiv 2510.26787. Cuts both ways: rapid relative growth (about 8x in a year) but about 79% of projects still fail.
- [medium/vendor_reported] **Insiders at an AI lab expect far more capability than the US public**: Nobel-level discovery feasible by 2030: 91% of Anthropic staff vs 39% of US adults. 'AI never able to do task': at most 2% of staff vs 19-40% of adults. Share of own work AI can do by 2030: staff average 75% vs US workers 28%. Model-implied 2030 GDP growth (median): public 5.4%, staff 17.1% (2026-10-16). [The AI expectations gap (Anthropic)](https://www.anthropic.com/research/ai-expectations-gap). Public sample: Morning Consult opt-in panel, N=10,980, Aug 11-23 2026, weighted. Staff poll: N=1,334, internal, unweighted. The lab has an interest in the result; staff may be biased upward. A re-field of 7,613 adults gave 44% on the Nobel question.
- [high/primary] **Most Americans think AI performs worse than people at common tasks, though the gap is narrowing**: AI rated worse than people on every task asked in both 2023 and 2026. 'Worse' share down 7 pts (driving), 6 (financial advice), 5 (medical advice). 'AI better' for homework rose from 26% to 29%. 'More harm than good': 39% (2026) vs 31% (2025). 'More good than harm': 9% (2026-07-28 (fielded May 4-11, 2026)). [Americans Cool Toward AI (Bentley University-Gallup)](https://news.gallup.com/poll/712751/americans-cool-toward.aspx). N=3,270, probability-based Gallup Panel, MOE ±2.4. The article does not list the full per-task percentages.
- [medium/primary] **Americans are split or skeptical on whether AI currently thinks better than people**: Economist/YouGov, May 29-Jun 1, 2026: AI reasoning worse than humans 43%, same 21%, better 17%. YouGov, Jul 16-20, 2026 (N=1,110): AI thinks better than the average person 33%, worse 29%, same 25%. Facts: 44% better / 18% worse. Complex reasoning: 33% / 28%. Creativity: 25% / 37% (2026-06 and 2026-08-01). [YouGov: Economist/YouGov poll; 'Do Americans think AI is smarter than Donald Trump?'](https://yougov.com/en-us/articles/55289-do-americans-think-ai-is-smarter-than-donald-trump). The two polls use different wording, so they are not directly comparable. Economist poll URL: https://yougov.com/en-us/articles/54888-more-expect-artificial-intelligence-ai-affect-economy-negatively-than-positively-americans-split-how-ai-will-impact-their-lives-may-29-june-1-2026-economist-yougov-poll
- [high/primary] **Most AI users in nine countries rely on free tiers, and free users rarely use agents**: 10% pay for AI themselves, 9% via work or school, 18% with any paid access. Agent used for a real task in the last week: 39% of paid vs 11% of free-only users (daily users: 50% vs 21%). Weekly generative-AI use (six countries): 51% in 2026 vs 34% in 2025. Daily: 28% vs 16% (2025) and 7% (2024). Weekly use by tool: ChatGPT 34%, Gemini 24%, Copilot 11%, Claude 7% (2026-10-29 (fieldwork Aug 14-Sep 28, 2026)). [Generative AI and News Report 2026 (Reuters Institute, Oxford; YouGov fieldwork)](https://reutersinstitute.politics.ox.ac.uk/generative-ai-and-news-report-2026-standalone-tools-information-infrastructure). About 2,000 per country, non-probability online sample. 10+9 does not equal 18 because of overlap. The report states that most users rely on free versions that are likely not the latest models. Literacy questions on AI's limitations were answered correctly only 60% of the time.
- [high/vendor_reported] **The paying share of ChatGPT users is small**: 900M weekly active users and 50M paying subscribers (about 5.6%) (2026-02-27). [ChatGPT reaches 900M weekly active users (TechCrunch, reporting an OpenAI blog post)](https://techcrunch.com/2026/02/27/chatgpt-reaches-900m-weekly-active-users). Later 1B (Aug 2026) and 1.2B (DevDay, Sep 29 2026) figures come mostly via secondary sources. The 1.2B may cover all OpenAI products. No updated subscriber count found.
- [high/primary] **US public concern about AI is at or near record highs and rising among young adults**: More concerned than excited: 52% (2026), 50% (2025), 51% (2024), 52% (2023), 37% (2021). More excited: 9%. Ages 18-29 more concerned: 55% (2026) vs 39% (2024). Expect fewer jobs in the next 20 years: 71% (2026) vs 64% (2024) (2026-08-18 (fielded June 22-28, 2026)). [Young adults in the U.S. are increasingly wary of AI (Pew Research Center)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2026/08/18/young-adults-in-the-us-are-increasingly-wary-of-ai-concerned-it-will-take-jobs/). N=3,488, American Trends Panel (probability-based).
- [high/primary] **AI experts and the US public diverge sharply on AI's impact**: Positive impact on the US over the next 20 years: experts 56% vs public 17%. More concerned than excited: public 51% vs experts 15%. Jobs positive: experts 73% vs public 23%. Economy: 69% vs 21%. Medical care: 84% vs 44% (2025-04-03 (public survey Aug 12-18, 2024, N=5,410; experts Aug 14-Oct 31, 2024, N=1,013)). [How the U.S. Public and AI Experts View Artificial Intelligence (Pew); also cited in the 2026 AI Index public opinion chapter](https://www.pewresearch.org/internet/2025/04/03/how-the-us-public-and-ai-experts-view-artificial-intelligence/). Expert responses are unweighted. The data are from 2024 and are being recycled into 2026 reports, including the AI Index.
- [medium/primary] **Global attitudes: slight rise in perceived benefits alongside rising nervousness**: Share saying AI products offer more benefits than drawbacks: 55% (2024) to 59% (2025). 'Nervous': 52%. US trust in its own government to regulate AI: 31% (lowest), global average 54%. 58% of employees worldwide use AI at work semiregularly or regularly (2026-04-13). [AI Index 2026, Chapter 9: Public Opinion (Stanford HAI)](https://hai.stanford.edu/ai-index/2026-ai-index-report/public-opinion). The chapter page doesn't name Ipsos; secondary coverage attributes the global numbers to Ipsos. Singapore's figure is inconsistent within the source (81% in text vs 91% in chart).
- [medium/primary] **Most Americans use AI-enabled products without recognizing them as AI**: Nearly all use AI-enabled products (maps, streaming, weather, assistants), but about two-thirds don't realize it. N≈4,000 US adults (2025-01). [Americans Use AI in Everyday Products Without Realizing It (Gallup-Telescope)](https://news.gallup.com/poll/654905/americans-everyday-products-without-realizing.aspx). Figures taken from the search summary and Axios coverage; the Gallup page was not fetched directly.
- [high/preprint] **Expert timelines for human-level AI shortened sharply between survey rounds**: AI Impacts 2023 survey (2,778 authors): 50% chance of HLMI by 2047, vs 2060 in the 2022 survey. Full automation of labor: 2116 (vs 2164) (2024-01 (arXiv 2401.02843)). [Thousands of AI Authors on the Future of AI (Grace et al., AI Impacts)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02843). No 2025 or 2026 edition found. The 69-year gap between the HLMI and full-automation framings is itself evidence that the AGI framing is ill-defined, which supports belief 3. Response rate about 15%.
- [medium/preprint] **ChatGPT use is mostly non-work**: About 700M weekly users and about 18B messages per week by July 2025. Non-work share of messages rose from 53% to more than 70% (2025-09 (NBER WP 34255)). [How People Use ChatGPT (Chatterji et al., OpenAI/NBER)](https://www.nber.org/papers/w34255). Co-authored by OpenAI staff. Figures from the abstract and secondary coverage.
- [medium/secondary_aggregator] **AI-washing has become a regulatory enforcement category**: SEC: Delphia ($225k penalty) and Global Predictions ($175k), Mar 2024. Presto Automation, Jan 2025 (first public company). SEC and DOJ charged Nate Inc. founder Albert Saniger in Apr 2025 for falsely claiming AI processed transactions. FTC: Operation AI Comply (Sep 2024); Air AI settlement Mar 2026 ($50k) (2024-2026). [Global Investigations Review 2026; DLA Piper (Feb 2026)](https://globalinvestigationsreview.com/review/the-investigations-review-of-the-americas/2026/article/us-enforcement-agencies-intensify-scrutiny-of-ai-washing). Sources conflict on FTC case counts since 2025. The FTC rescinded the Rytr order in Dec 2025. Verify against SEC press releases before citing.
- [medium/primary] **Open-weight models trail the closed frontier by a few months**: About 4 months on average since Jan 2026 on the Epoch Capabilities Index (about 8 ECI points); an earlier estimate was 3 months (2026). [Open models lag state-of-the-art closed models by 4 months (Epoch AI)](https://epoch.ai/data-insights/open-closed-eci-gap). Not a free-tier vs paid-tier comparison; no direct Epoch analysis of the free-tier gap was found.

### surprises

- Superforecasters in 2022 gave the benchmark outcomes that actually happened by 2025 an average probability of just 9.7%, and they put IMO gold around 2035. It happened in July 2025.
- Developers in METR's 2025 trial were measured 19% slower with AI but believed they were 20% faster.
- In METR's 2026 follow-up, 30-50% of developers refused to submit some tasks because they didn't want to do them without AI, and that wrecked the experiment.
