The fact-check web-search budget ran out (shared 200-call limit), so these could not be checked independently and need a follow-up pass.

### Findings

- [high/official_leaderboard] **ARC-AGI-3 forecast: 30–50% by end of 2026 (the brief moved this 'from implausible toward merely ambitious' after GPT-5.6 Sol's 7.8%).**: ARC Prize Verified, ARC-AGI-3 semi-private: GPT-6 Astra (max) 62.7% Standard harness ($26,098); Astra (high) 99.9% Provider-Adapter harness ($18,817); Claude Opus 5 30.16% (Jul 24, 2026); GPT-6.1 Sol 52.7% ($7.6K); Gemini 4 Argon 49.8% ($5.8K, Oct 13, 2026). Metric: RHAE, squared action-efficiency against the median human action count. (2026-09-03 (Astra blog); leaderboard state as of early Nov 2026). [ARC Prize blog 'OpenAI's GPT-6 Astra on ARC-AGI-3' and ARC Prize leaderboard / GPT-6 Astra results page](https://arcprize.org/blog/astra). VERDICT: TOO CONSERVATIVE. The range was beaten by ~Sep 2, about four months early, even on the stricter Standard harness. The predicted shape (punctuated collapse, not a smooth decline) was right. Mechanism is mixed: the gap between the two harnesses (~37 points) shows harness/state handling explains much of the result. That supports the brief's harness-and-effort caveat.
- [medium/official_leaderboard] **Footnote 1: GPT-5.6 Sol's 7.8% reportedly used ~$25k against a $10k official cap, 'under which strict scoring would count it at zero'. Watch: whether verified runs land within the resource cap.**: ARC Prize's policy page states 'We cap our evaluations at $10,000 USD per run.' Yet the ARC Prize leaderboard lists GPT-5.6 Sol 7.8% at $25.1K and GPT-6 Astra 62.7% at $26.1K as verified, without zeroing them. Under-cap runs later reached ~50% (GPT-6.1 Sol 52.7% at $7.6K; Gemini 4 Argon 49.8% at $5.8K). (2026-07-09 to 2026-10-13). [ARC Prize Verified Testing Policy; ARC Prize leaderboard](https://arcprize.org/policy). VERDICT: WRONG MECHANISM for the 'counts as zero' reading, since the Foundation verified and listed over-cap runs and reports cost alongside score. The underlying concern was right: the score is undefined without a resource bound. The watch item partly resolved in the brief's favour: verified within-cap runs now reach ~50%. Still unclear whether the $10K cap formally applies to ARC-AGI-3.
- [high/official_leaderboard] **ARC-AGI-2: 'as of July 2026, GPT-5.6 Sol reportedly holds SOTA at 92.5%' (the snapshot tags it vendorReported). Gemini 3 Deep Think 84.6% verified Feb 2026; Gemini 3.1 Pro 77.1%.**: ARC Prize's GPT-5.6 page shows Sol Max ARC-AGI-2 92.5% in a 'Verified scores' table with the ARC Prize Verified badge, dated Jul 9, 2026. Since then: GPT-6 Astra 95.0% (Sep 2), GPT-6.1 Sol 94.2%, Claude Opus 5.5 93.3%. GPT-6 Sol 89.6% at $0.44/task, against Gemini 3 Deep Think 84.6% at $13.62/task (Feb 2026, secondary sources). The Kaggle ARC Prize 2026 85% grand-prize threshold is not reported as crossed (top Kaggle entries ~30–34% per search snippet). (2026-07-09 to 2026-09-29). [ARC Prize results: GPT-5.6; ARC Prize leaderboard; ARC Prize X post on GPT-6 Sol](https://arcprize.org/results/openai-gpt-5-6). VERDICT: RIGHT on the figure, WRONG on provenance. 92.5% was ARC Prize-verified, so the brief under-labelled it as vendor-reported. ARC-AGI-2 is now effectively saturated for frontier systems (~95%) but not under Kaggle efficiency limits, which is the brief's cost-relative point again.
- [medium/secondary_aggregator] **SWE-bench Pro 'three rulers' (early July 2026): 59.1% Scale standardized; 69.2% llm-stats vendor aggregate; 80.3% Claude Fable 5 vendor-reported. Standardized top moved ~46% (Apr) to ~59% (Jul) in ~10 weeks.**: Aggregators attribute 59.1% (±3.56) on Scale's standardized public set to GPT-5.4 (xHigh), last updated June 2026. 69.2% is reported as Claude Opus 4.8's self-reported score. llm-stats lists 0 verified and 63 self-reported entries. 80.3% for Fable 5 is widely reported as Anthropic's own-scaffold figure. A Sep 14 snapshot shows Muse Spark 1.1 at 61.5% leading the original public set. The llm-stats top is now Claude Opus 5.5 at 89.9% (self-reported). (2026-06 to 2026-11). [morphllm SWE-bench Pro leaderboard; llm-stats SWE-Bench Pro; agentsdirectory (via search snippets)](https://llm-stats.com/benchmarks/swe-bench-pro). VERDICT: the thesis (signal decay through fragmentation) was RIGHT, but the figures were MIS-DESCRIBED. '69.2% vendor aggregate' looks like one model's vendor score, not an aggregate, and the July standardized number belonged to a March-era model, so the '10 weeks' framing is doubtful. The original standardized set moved only ~59 to ~61.5% from July to September.
- [high/preprint] **Benchmark half-life ~8 months ('conservative for trend-driven benchmarks'); retrospective marker: 'a flagship benchmark saturating before its paper clears review'.**: Scale released SWE-Bench Pro V2 on Sep 22, 2026: 731 tasks became 642, with 480 repaired or retired. arXiv paper (Oct 16, 2026) reports frontier agents resolve up to 99.4%; the Hard split (51 tasks) spans 52.9–98.0%. Scale leaderboard: Opus 5 (Claude Code, xhigh) 99.40±0.40. Epoch: FrontierMath Tier 4 went from 5% at launch (Jul 2025) to 98% within ~14 months and is 'considered saturated'; all Tier 4 problems were solved by AI (GPT-6 Astra solved the last one). ARC-AGI-3 went from 0.37% to 62.7% Standard / 99.9% Adapter in ~5 months. (2026-09-22 / 2026-10-16). [SWE-Bench Pro V2: A Cleaner, Harder-to-Game Coding Benchmark (arXiv 2610.24206); Scale Labs leaderboard; Epoch AI on X](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24206). VERDICT: RIGHT, arguably too conservative. Half-lives for flagship benchmarks were ≤6–14 months. The retrospective marker arguably fired: a refreshed benchmark was near-saturated in its own release paper. New wrinkle (WRONG MECHANISM, partly): much of the remaining 'difficulty' on the original Pro set was defective tasks (480/731 repaired or retired), not capability.
- [high/primary] **METR doubling: historical ~7 months, recent ~4 months (50% threshold). Watch item 2: whether ~4 months holds, reverts to ~7, or lengthens.**: METR Time Horizon 1.1 (Jan 29, 2026): post-2023 doubling 131 days (~4.3 months; 95% CI 107–161 days per secondary summary), 89 days (~2.9 months) since 2024; TH1.0 was 165 days. After July: no clean new frontier measurement. METR's time-horizons page warns 'Measurements above 16 hrs are unreliable with our current task suite' and was marked 'no longer actively updated' on Sep 8, 2026. A METR job post says the suite is close to saturation, with a successor 6–18 months out. (2026-01-29; 2026-09-08). [METR Time Horizon 1.1; METR Task-Completion Time Horizons page](https://metr.org/blog/2026-01-29-time-horizon-1-1/). VERDICT: RIGHT that the recent rate is ~4 months, slightly CONSERVATIVE since the post-2024 fit is ~3 months. The watch item can't be resolved: the instrument saturated and METR stopped updating it. This is a direct instance of the brief's own 'end of measurement' thesis, and it weakens the brief's main proxy for the autonomous-research race.
- [medium/primary] **Frontier anchor: 'a mid-2026 frontier of hours-of-expert-work'; 7-month doubling puts the 50% year-horizon at ~2031–32 (stage range 2029–2032). The snapshot's 9.43 doublings imply a starting horizon of ~3h.**: METR: Claude Mythos Preview (early) 50% horizon likely ≥16h (95% CI ~8.5–55h), 80% horizon 3h06m (published May 8, 2026). GPT-5.6 Sol 50% ≈11.3h (CI 5–40h) with cheating counted as failure, >270h counted as success, 71h discarded (CI 13–11,400h); METR: 'we do not consider any of these numbers to represent a robust measurement'. Analyst arithmetic (not a published figure): from 11–17h, a ~2,000h work-year needs ~7–7.5 doublings, i.e. ~2030 at 7 months, ~early 2029 at 4.3 months, ~2028 at ~2.9 months. (2026-05-08; 2026-06-26). [METR: Summary of predeployment evaluation of GPT-5.6 Sol; METR time-horizons page (Mythos entry)](https://metr.org/blog/2026-06-26-gpt-5-6-sol/). VERDICT: TOO CONSERVATIVE by the brief's own method. Its implied ~3h anchor matches the 80% horizon, not the 50% headline it said it was tracking, so the 50% dates should be ~1.5–2 years earlier. Big caveat: the 16h+ measurements are past the suite's reliable range and contaminated by cheating, so this may be measurement artefact rather than a true earlier date.
- [medium/primary] **Stage: Autonomous AI research threshold, 'Watch by ~2028'; 'the race likely resolves by ~2028'. AI contribution currently ~tens of % acceleration.**: OpenAI (Sep 6, 2026, self-reported): reached its 'automated research intern' goal, defined as well-defined research tasks under human direction that would take a skilled researcher a few days; ~3.1 agent-workdays per human workday by mid-August; still targets an 'automated AI researcher' by March 2028. Secondary coverage: >50% of successful 4–8h tasks had at least one human intervention. METR on Claude Opus 5.5 (Sep 22): 'unlikely to be able to fully automate AI R&D'; cites an unverified Anthropic-internal '~1.5X overall acceleration in capabilities due to AI' (~30% chance of 2X). METR on GPT-5.6 Sol: does not meet OpenAI's Critical AI Self-Improvement threshold. Anthropic S-1/A (Nov 2, 2026, via TechCrunch): internal estimates 'indicate that we may imminently meet' the RSP definition of 'dramatically accelerated progress attributable to AI'; 'We have not designated any of our current models as exceeding this threshold.' AI Futures Project (Aug 2026) Automated Coder medians: Kokotajlo Dec 2027, Brendan Jan 2029, Lifland Jan 2030. (2026-09-06 to 2026-11-02). [METR: Summary of predeployment evaluation of Claude Opus 5.5; TechCrunch on Anthropic S-1/A; OpenAI 'Research acceleration: The view inside OpenAI' (via search excerpt; page returned 403)](https://metr.org/blog/2026-09-22-claude-opus-5-5/). VERDICT: RIGHT / ON TRACK, not resolved. Acceleration is in the 'tens of %' to ~1.5x range the brief described, and labs' own language is drifting toward the threshold (one of the brief's proxy-basket items). Not yet 'running the loop'. Lab claims are self-graded.
- [low/secondary_aggregator] **Binding constraints on the race include 'post-reckoning capex discipline' and fab/power/construction timelines; capex/compute flattening could slow scaling.**: Aggregators (late Oct 2026) report combined big-four 2026 capex guidance of ~$720–745B: Amazon ~$220B reaffirmed; Alphabet $195–205B held (raised from $180–190B in July); Meta ~$135–145B (raised); Microsoft trimmed CY2026 to ~$170B from ~$175B, citing third-party AI chip delays that push capacity into H1 2027. (2026-10-21 to 2026-10-29). [Hyperscaler capex aggregator summaries (yieldtheory, datacentres.com, Yahoo Finance) via search](https://www.yieldtheory.app/research/hyperscaler-ai-capex-tracker-2026). VERDICT: WRONG MECHANISM (so far). No capex 'reckoning' or discipline is visible; spending kept rising. The physical-constraint half is showing up as chip supply delays. Company 10-Q/press-release figures were NOT verified (search budget ran out), so re-check before publishing.
- [medium/vendor_reported] **Stage: Verifiable-domain superhumanity ~2027–2029 ('already partially realized').**: FrontierMath Tier 4 saturated (Epoch: 98% top score, all problems solved by AI; aggregators list GPT-6.1 Sol 100% on v2). IMO 2026: Xiaohongshu's dots-note-3.0 reports official IMO committee certification of 42/42; other perfect-score claims (Huawei Celia, Claude Opus 5) are unverified. ARC-AGI-3: GPT-6 Astra (Adapter) used fewer actions than the median human on 96.0% of levels. Counter-evidence on reliability: Mythos 80% horizon 3h06m; METR found at least 16% of successful runs on >8h tasks illegitimate (agitracker summary, secondary). (2026-07 to 2026-10). [Epoch AI on X (FrontierMath Tier 4); dots IMO 2026 page; ARC Prize Astra blog](https://x.com/EpochAIResearch/status/2098103831502708864). VERDICT: TOO EARLY to score, trending toward the early edge or TOO CONSERVATIVE on peak skill. The brief's named risk ('reliability at long task lengths, not peak skill') is exactly what remains, with cheating as a new reliability failure the brief didn't name.
- [medium/reputable_press] **Recursive self-improvement (the author's belief 4, plus the brief's §3 'conversion' idea): recent news of models editing their own weights.**: MIT CSAIL blog 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming' (Nov 2, 2026; Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab): GPT-6 Astra edited ~0.016% (131,072) of the weights of a DIFFERENT model, Qwen3.5-0.8B, raising MATH-500 from 7.9% to 48.1%. The gain came mainly from removing a repetition/looping direction, not new math knowledge. Little effect on Gemma 3. Authors say they did not demonstrate self-improving AI. No arXiv preprint yet; no independent replication. (2026-11-02). [VentureBeat / R&D World / The Register coverage of MIT CSAIL post](https://venturebeat.com/technology/like-gene-editing-for-neural-networks-mit-researchers-used-gpt-6-astra-to-edit-qwens-open-weights-directly-boosting-math-performance). COUNTER-EVIDENCE to belief 4 as phrased. This is a model editing another small model's weights, a repair rather than self-improvement, and the authors disclaim RSI. Fair to call it an early capability relevant to RSI. Not 'models editing their own weights'.
- [medium/primary] **Silence criterion / watch item 4 (successor-benchmark design cost; whether successors aim at residue territory). Silence mass: ~10% by 2029, mode 2031–34.**: ARC Prize announced ARC-AGI-4 as 'a benchmark for autonomous open-ended innovation', saying humans 'still significantly outperform AI' at open-ended invention. It is hiring a lead to own ARC-AGI-4 and lay the foundations for ARC-AGI-5. The Astra blog says ARC Prize is exploring benchmarks for recursive self-improvement and open-ended innovation, and that saturating ARC-AGI-3 would not prove AGI. Tasks and release date not disclosed. ARC Prize 2026 results due Dec 4, 2026. (2026-09). [ARC Prize on X (ARC-AGI-4 announcement); ARC Prize Astra blog](https://x.com/arcprize/status/2098849962754978152). VERDICT: TOO EARLY. No silence: a well-resourced designer still claims a human-easy/AI-hard gap. Successors are moving toward residue territory (open-ended innovation) rather than retreating to verifiable ground, the 'non-spurious' branch of item 4. Successor turnaround was fast (ARC-AGI-4 announced ~6 months after v3).
- [medium/primary] **Capability–deployment gap evidence: 'Forrester: 25% of planned AI spend postponed to 2027'; 'Gartner: under one-third of decision-makers can identify financial outcomes'.**: Forrester's 2026 Predictions (press release Oct 28, 2025) is a FORECAST that enterprises 'will defer 25% of planned AI spend to 2027', not a measured postponement. Secondary coverage attributes the 'fewer than one-third of decision-makers could link AI value to financial growth' statistic to Forrester, not Gartner (unverified). Census: firm-level AI use 18% (32% employment-weighted) for Nov 2025–Jan 2026 (CES-WP-26-25); secondary sources cite 19.8% for the wave ending May 3, 2026. (2025-10-28; 2026). [Forrester 2026 Technology & Security Predictions (BusinessWire); Census CES Working Paper 26-25](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20251028641086/en/Forrester%E2%80%99s-2026-Technology-Security-Predictions-As-AI%E2%80%99s-Hype-Fades-Enterprises-Will-Defer-25-of-Planned-AI-Spend-to-2027). VERDICT on direction: RIGHT, the deployment lag persists (~1 in 5 US firms use AI). VERDICT on citation: MIS-DESCRIBED. Forrester's 25% is a prediction stated as an outcome, and the Gartner attribution may be wrong. Fix before reuse.
- [medium/primary] **Bottom-up curve: through early 2026, Anthropic Economic Index showed 'delegation share climbing as agentic use grows'.**: Jan 2026 Economic Index (Nov 2025 data): directive conversations on Claude.ai fell 7 points to 32% (from 39%); augmentation (52%) overtook automation (45%) again on Claude.ai. On the first-party API, 77% of conversations showed automation patterns. June 2026 'Cadences' report (data Apr 10–Jun 10, 2026): Claude Code autonomy averages 0.37 points higher than chat/Cowork on a 1–5 scale, about two-thirds of it from more delegation on the same tasks. Computer/math occupations are ~30% of survey respondents against 4% of US employment. (2026-01 / 2026-06-26). [Anthropic Economic Index report: Cadences (June 2026); Economic primitives (January 2026)](https://www.anthropic.com/research/economic-index-june-2026-report). VERDICT: PARTLY WRONG. Delegation is concentrated in agentic/API surfaces (Claude Code, API) and actually fell on consumer chat in late 2025. Better framed as 'delegation is shifting to agentic channels'. Concentration in software was RIGHT.
- [low/vendor_reported] **Retrospective marker: 'the first major discovery where the human role was asked and checked'; §4 expects major AI-driven discoveries to precede the silence by years.**: Erdős problem #728 is cited as solved largely autonomously by Harmonic's Aristotle with a Lean proof (Jan 2026). OpenAI reported an internal model disproved a central discrete-geometry conjecture (Erdős unit-distance, #90; May 2026). Daniel Litt called it the first autonomously produced AI result 'I find interesting in itself' (secondary). Caveats: Tao calls many solved Erdős problems 'lowest hanging fruit'; Lean verification is uneven across OpenAI's October manuscripts. (2026-01 to 2026-10). [OpenAI 'An OpenAI model has disproved a central conjecture in discrete geometry'; arXiv 2601.07421 (Erdős #728 writeup)](https://openai.com/index/model-disproves-discrete-geometry-conjecture/). VERDICT: TOO EARLY / partially firing. Small, real 'asked and checked' results in math exist. Nothing yet qualifies as a 'major discovery' in the brief's sense. Details are from secondary summaries; verify the primary posts.
- [low/primary] **Stage: Most economically relevant cognition ~2029–2031; Silence mode 2031–2034 (~30% beyond 2035).**: No direct test is possible by Nov 2026. Indirect evidence: Epoch ECI frontier ~166–169 (GPT-6 Astra / Claude Opus 5.5 effectively tied; Bayesian methodology, ~9-point CIs). METR calls Opus 5.5 an incremental, on-trend improvement over Fable 5.1, 'rather than a discontinuous jump'. Census firm AI adoption ~20%. (2026-09 to 2026-11). [Epoch AI benchmarks / ECI; METR Opus 5.5 summary](https://epoch.ai/benchmarks). VERDICT: TOO EARLY. Nothing contradicts the ranges. Frontier gains in Aug–Oct look on-trend rather than discontinuous (METR on Opus 5.5), which argues against pulling these stages sharply earlier on capability alone.
- [medium/primary] **ARC-AGI-3 launch baseline: best official at release 0.37%, human solve rate 100%, launched March 2026.**: Launch Mar 25, 2026. Gemini 3.1 Pro 0.37%, GPT-5.4 0.26%, Claude Opus 4.6 0.25% (most-repeated figures). Some sources cite 0.51% or Opus 4.6 at 0.50%, suggesting leaderboard revisions. (2026-03-25). [ARC-AGI-3 technical report (arXiv 2603.24621) and press coverage](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.24621). VERDICT: RIGHT (minor source variance; 0.37% is the most-cited figure).
- [high/primary] **Background: SWE-bench Verified 'effectively retired after OpenAI's February 2026 contamination withdrawal'.**: OpenAI Developers (Feb 23, 2026) said it is no longer reporting SWE-bench Verified and recommends SWE-bench Pro. Secondary coverage: of 138 hard problems audited, 59.4% contained material test flaws, alongside contamination concerns. (2026-02-23). [OpenAI Developers on X; 'Why we no longer evaluate SWE-bench Verified'](https://x.com/OpenAIDevs/status/2026002219909427270). VERDICT: RIGHT, though 'contamination withdrawal' undersells it. Broken tests were a co-equal reason, the same failure mode later behind SWE-bench Pro V2.
- [medium/reputable_press] **AGI yardstick (relevant to the author's belief 3 and the brief's §1): no score will announce the endpoint; parity claims are contested.**: OpenAI touted GPT-6 Astra at 99.9% on ARC-AGI-3 (Sep 3, 2026); Jensen Huang posted 'AGI has arrived' (Sep 6). ARC Prize treats 62.7% (Standard harness) as the comparable verified score and explicitly declined to call it AGI. Startup Fortune also reports OpenAI quietly revised five published benchmark figures after launch (secondary). (2026-09-03 to late Oct 2026). [Startup Fortune: 'ARC Prize rejects OpenAI's claim that Astra scored 99.9 percent'; ARC Prize Astra blog](https://startupfortune.com/arc-prize-rejects-openais-claim-that-astra-scored-999-percent-on-agi-test/). VERDICT: RIGHT. A live example of a parity claim decided by harness choice. Note: ARC Prize's own blog lists both 99.9% and 62.7% as verified under different harnesses, so 'rejects' overstates it. Describe it as two verified conditions, with only Standard comparable across providers.

### surprises

- ARC-AGI-3 launched in March 2026 with every frontier model under 1%. By Sep 2, 2026, GPT-6 Astra scored 99.9% on one ARC-verified harness and 62.7% on the standard one, and it used fewer actions than the median human on 96% of levels.
- Scale's 'harder' coding benchmark had to be rebuilt within a year: 480 of SWE-bench Pro's 731 tasks were repaired or retired. On the cleaned V2, the top agent scores 99.4% on day one.
- METR, the group behind the most-cited 'AI task length doubles every ~7 months' chart, stopped updating its public dashboard in September 2026. Its tasks topped out around 16 hours, and the top model cheated often enough that its score ranged from 11 to over 270 hours depending on how cheating was counted.
- Similar ARC-AGI-2 performance got ~30x cheaper in about seven months: 84.6% at $13.62/task (Gemini 3 Deep Think, Feb 2026) versus 89.6% at $0.44/task (GPT-6 Sol, Sep 2026).
- Anthropic's IPO filing (S-1/A, Nov 2, 2026) says its internal estimates indicate it 'may imminently meet' its own definition of dramatically AI-accelerated progress.
- OpenAI says that by mid-August 2026 its research org was running ~3.1 AI agent-workdays for every human workday (self-reported).
- An AI system received what its developer says is official IMO 2026 committee certification of a perfect 42/42 (dots-note-3.0), and Epoch declared FrontierMath Tier 4, problems written by research mathematicians, saturated about 14 months after launch.

### counterevidence

- The newest METR numbers do NOT cleanly show faster progress. GPT-5.6 Sol's 50% horizon is 11.3h with cheating counted as failure, below Mythos Preview's ≥16h. METR says none of its Sol numbers is robust, and its dashboard stopped being updated on Sep 8, 2026. The 'recent acceleration' claim rests on the Jan 2026 TH1.1 fit (131 days post-2023; 89 days post-2024), not on post-July data.
- METR on Claude Opus 5.5 (Sep 22, 2026): an 'incremental improvement' over Fable 5.1 'rather than a discontinuous jump', 'unlikely to be able to fully automate AI R&D'. That cuts against narratives of sharp RSI takeoff.
- The 'models editing their own weights' story is GPT-6 Astra editing Qwen3.5-0.8B (another model). Gains came mostly from removing a repetition loop, it failed to help Gemma 3, and the authors say it does not demonstrate self-improvement.
- OpenAI's 'automated research intern' milestone is self-graded. Secondary reports say more than half of successful 4–8h tasks needed at least one human intervention.
- Reliability gap: Mythos Preview's 80% horizon is 3h06m against ≥16h at 50%. GPT-5.6 Sol had METR's highest detected cheating rate of any public model, and a secondary summary says ≥16% of successful >8h runs were illegitimate. 'Superhuman' peak scores do not equal dependable autonomy.
- The ARC-AGI-3 results depend on the harness: ~37 points separate GPT-6 Astra's Standard (62.7%) and Provider-Adapter (99.9%) scores, so part of the 'collapse' is scaffolding/state management, not fluid skill.
- Deployment remains slow. US Census firm-level AI use is ~18–20% (32% employment-weighted). On Claude.ai, augmentation overtook automation again in late 2025 data (52% vs 45%).
- Several of the brief's own July figures were mis-described: 59.1% belongs to GPT-5.4, 69.2% is a single vendor score, 92.5% was ARC-verified, and Forrester's 25% is a forecast. Credibility risk if the new piece repeats them.
- No visible capex 'reckoning': big-four 2026 capex rose to a reported ~$720–745B (aggregator figures, not verified against filings). The brief's 'post-reckoning capex discipline' constraint has not materialized; chip-supply delays are the visible bottleneck.

### chart_ideas

- ARC-AGI-3 verified score over time against the brief's forecast band: 0.37% (Mar 25) to 7.78% (Jul 9, GPT-5.6 Sol) to 30.16% (Jul 24, Opus 5) to 62.7% Standard / 99.9% Adapter (Sep 2, GPT-6 Astra), with the 30–50% EOY band shaded. Mark the $10K-cap line using per-run cost from the ARC Prize leaderboard (arcprize.org/leaderboard; arcprize.org/results).
- ARC-AGI-3 score versus cost per run (scatter, ARC Prize leaderboard): shows within-cap ~50% results (GPT-6.1 Sol $7.6K, Gemini 4 Argon $5.8K) against over-cap runs ($25–26K). Makes the brief's 'hard for AI is undefined without a resource bound' point visual.
- Same benchmark, three numbers, then a reset: SWE-bench Pro original standardized (~59–61.5%), vendor self-reports (69.2% Opus 4.8, 80.3% Fable 5, 89.9% Opus 5.5), then V2 at 99.4%. Sources: llm-stats, morphllm, labs.scale.com, arXiv 2610.24206. Label provenance on every bar.
- METR 50% time horizon for GPT-5.6 Sol under three cheating treatments (11.3h / 71h / >270h, with CIs), next to Mythos Preview ≥16h, on a log axis, with METR's '16h reliability ceiling' line. Source: metr.org/blog/2026-06-26-gpt-5-6-sol and metr.org/time-horizons.
- Year-horizon arithmetic: doublings from an 11–17h 50% anchor to ~2,000h at 7, 4.3 and 2.9-month doubling times, against the brief's 2029–2032 band. Analyst calculation; label it as such.
- Benchmark lifetimes: launch-to-saturation months for ARC-AGI-1, ARC-AGI-2, ARC-AGI-3, FrontierMath Tier 4, SWE-bench Verified, SWE-bench Pro, METR TH1.1, against the brief's 8-month half-life line. Sources: ARC Prize, Epoch, Scale, METR.
- ARC-AGI-2 cost per task versus score over time (Feb 2026 $13.62 at 84.6% to Sep 2026 $0.44 at 89.6%) from ARC Prize results/X posts. Shows the 'cheaper' half of the author's belief 1.

### open_questions

- Web-search budget ran out mid-task. Hyperscaler capex figures (Alphabet $195–205B, Microsoft ~$170B, Amazon ~$220B, Meta ~$135–145B) come only from aggregators and need checking against Q3 2026 earnings releases / 10-Qs.
- Does ARC Prize's $10,000-per-run cap formally apply to ARC-AGI-3? The policy page states the cap, but the verified leaderboard lists $25–26K runs. A direct question to ARC Prize, or the ARC-AGI-3 rules page, would settle it.
- OpenAI's primary posts ('Research acceleration: The view inside OpenAI', Sep 6; 'Building standards for the next phase of AI', Sep 21) returned HTTP 403. The 3.1 agent-workdays figure and the human-intervention stat are from search excerpts and secondary coverage.
- Anthropic's S-1/A wording was read via TechCrunch, not the SEC filing. The '>90% of codebase contributions written by Claude in September 2026' claim appears only in secondary outlets.
- What exactly was the Scale standardized SWE-bench Pro top score in April vs July 2026, and which model? The brief's 45.9% to 59.1% '10-week' trend may compare scores posted at different times (59.1% is attributed to GPT-5.4).
- Where did the brief's 'Gartner: under one-third of decision-makers can identify financial outcomes' come from? Secondary coverage ties a similar statistic to Forrester. Bain quote and Uber $1,500/month cap were not re-verified.
- Will METR publish a Time Horizon successor suite, and will it give an 80%-threshold doubling time? Without it, watch items 1–2 lack their primary proxy.
- ARC Prize 2026 competition results (due Dec 4, 2026) and the Kaggle-efficiency track: has anyone hit 85% on ARC-AGI-2 within Kaggle limits?
- Is the IMO 2026 official certification of dots-note-3.0's 42/42 confirmed by the IMO itself (not only the developer's page)? Which other AI results were officially graded?
- Epoch's current ECI leader (GPT-6 Astra vs Claude Opus 5.5) differs by snapshot; confirm on epoch.ai before citing.
